## 34. Transcription Policy

Jangan hard-code.

```sql
CREATE TABLE transcription_policy (
    id                              UUID PRIMARY KEY,
    name                            VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL,
    job_type                         VARCHAR(50) NOT NULL,

    provider_name                    VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL,
    language_code                    VARCHAR(50),

    enable_speaker_diarization       BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT FALSE,
    expected_speaker_count           INTEGER,

    enable_word_timestamps           BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT TRUE,
    enable_punctuation               BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT TRUE,
    enable_profanity_filter          BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT FALSE,
    enable_pii_redaction             BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT FALSE,

    mean_confidence_threshold         NUMERIC(6,5),
    segment_confidence_threshold      NUMERIC(6,5),

    max_duration_ms                   BIGINT,
    max_file_size_bytes               BIGINT,

    audio_normalization_strategy      VARCHAR(100),
    chunk_duration_ms                 BIGINT,
    chunk_overlap_ms                  BIGINT,

    created_at                        TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
    updated_at                        TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),

    UNIQUE (name)
);
```

Policy examples:

| Policy | Use Case | Key Options |
|---|---|---|
| `MEETING_TRANSCRIPT_ID` | meeting Bahasa Indonesia | diarization on, punctuation on |
| `CALL_CENTER_STEREO` | call recording | channel separation, diarization off |
| `EVIDENCE_AUDIO` | legal/case evidence | preserve raw, review threshold strict |
| `VIDEO_CAPTION` | video subtitle | word timestamps + SRT/VTT |
| `INTERNAL_NOTE` | simple audio note | no review, cheaper provider |

## 36. MyBatis Mapper

```java
public interface TranscriptionResultMapper {

    void insertResult(TranscriptionResultRecord record);

    void insertSegments(@Param("segments") List<TranscriptSegmentRecord> segments);

    void insertWords(@Param("words") List<TranscriptWordRecord> words);

    Optional<TranscriptionResultRecord> findByJobId(@Param("jobId") UUID jobId);

    List<TranscriptSegmentRecord> findSegments(
            @Param("resultId") UUID resultId,
            @Param("fromMs") Long fromMs,
            @Param("toMs") Long toMs
    );
}
```

Batch insert:

```xml
<insert id="insertSegments">
    INSERT INTO transcript_segment (
        id,
        transcription_result_id,
        segment_index,
        speaker_label,
        channel_label,
        start_ms,
        end_ms,
        text,
        confidence,
        metadata_json
    )
    VALUES
    <foreach collection="segments" item="s" separator=",">
        (
            #{s.id},
            #{s.transcriptionResultId},
            #{s.segmentIndex},
            #{s.speakerLabel},
            #{s.channelLabel},
            #{s.startMs},
            #{s.endMs},
            #{s.text},
            #{s.confidence},
            #{s.metadataJson}::jsonb
        )
    </foreach>
    ON CONFLICT (transcription_result_id, segment_index) DO NOTHING
</insert>
```

## 37. Audio Preparation Worker

```java
public final class AudioPreparationService {

    private final FfprobeMetadataExtractor metadataExtractor;
    private final FfmpegAudioExtractor audioExtractor;
    private final ObjectStorageClient storageClient;
    private final MediaAssetRepository assetRepository;

    public AudioPreparationResult prepare(MediaJob job, MediaAsset sourceAsset) {
        MediaMetadata metadata = metadataExtractor.probe(sourceAsset);

        if (!metadata.hasAudioStream()) {
            throw new PermanentMediaException("AUDIO_STREAM_MISSING");
        }

        TranscriptionPolicy policy = resolvePolicy(job);

        if (metadata.durationMs() > policy.maxDurationMs()) {
            throw new PermanentMediaException("AUDIO_TOO_LONG");
        }

        if (metadata.isAudio() && metadata.isProviderCompatible(policy)) {
            return AudioPreparationResult.useOriginal(sourceAsset, metadata);
        }

        Path localInput = storageClient.downloadToTemp(sourceAsset.objectRef());

        Path normalizedAudio = audioExtractor.extractAndNormalize(
                localInput,
                policy.audioNormalizationStrategy()
        );

        MediaAsset normalizedAsset = storageClient.uploadDerivedAudio(
                job,
                sourceAsset,
                normalizedAudio
        );

        return AudioPreparationResult.normalized(normalizedAsset, metadata);
    }
}
```

---

## 38. FFmpeg Wrapper Safety

```java
public final class FfmpegAudioExtractor {

    public Path extractToWav(Path input, Path output) {
        ProcessBuilder pb = new ProcessBuilder(
                "ffmpeg",
                "-y",
                "-i", input.toAbsolutePath().toString(),
                "-vn",
                "-ac", "1",
                "-ar", "16000",
                "-c:a", "pcm_s16le",
                output.toAbsolutePath().toString()
        );

        pb.redirectErrorStream(false);

        try {
            Process process = pb.start();

            boolean finished = process.waitFor(5, TimeUnit.MINUTES);

            if (!finished) {
                process.destroyForcibly();
                throw new AudioPreparationException("FFmpeg timed out");
            }

            if (process.exitValue() != 0) {
                String stderr = new String(
                        process.getErrorStream().readAllBytes(),
                        StandardCharsets.UTF_8
                );
                throw new AudioPreparationException("FFmpeg failed: " + stderr);
            }

            return output;

        } catch (IOException e) {
            throw new AudioPreparationException("Failed to start FFmpeg", e);
        } catch (InterruptedException e) {
            Thread.currentThread().interrupt();
            throw new AudioPreparationException("Interrupted while running FFmpeg", e);
        }
    }
}
```

Security rules:

```text
- use ProcessBuilder args, not shell string
- set timeout
- run in resource-limited container
- input path controlled by service
- temp directory private
- cleanup files
- log command template, not sensitive URL
```

---

## 39. Error Classification

| Error Class | Example | Retry? |
|---|---|---:|
| `AUDIO_STREAM_MISSING` | video has no audio | No |
| `UNSUPPORTED_AUDIO_CODEC` | codec unsupported and transcode failed | No/limited |
| `AUDIO_TOO_LONG` | duration exceeds policy | No |
| `AUDIO_TOO_LARGE` | file exceeds policy | No |
| `FFMPEG_TIMEOUT` | extraction stuck | Limited |
| `FFMPEG_EXIT_ERROR` | invalid/corrupt media | Depends |
| `PROVIDER_RATE_LIMIT` | quota limit | Yes |
| `PROVIDER_TIMEOUT` | provider submit/fetch timeout | Yes |
| `PROVIDER_JOB_FAILED` | provider rejected audio | No/depends |
| `TRANSCRIPT_EMPTY` | no speech detected | Review/complete as empty depending policy |
| `LOW_CONFIDENCE` | result poor | Review |
| `RESULT_PARSE_FAILED` | provider schema parse error | Limited |
| `STORAGE_RESULT_MISSING` | output not found yet | Retry |
| `LANGUAGE_UNSUPPORTED` | invalid language for provider | No |

---

## 40. Empty Transcript Handling

Empty transcript can mean:

```text
- actual silence
- wrong audio stream
- unsupported language
- volume too low
- noise only
- provider failure
- incorrect normalization
```

Decision:

| Evidence | Action |
|---|---|
| silence detection confirms silence | complete with `NO_SPEECH_DETECTED` |
| audio energy exists but transcript empty | review/retry provider |
| language unknown | retry language detection / review |
| provider error | classify failure |
| no audio stream | reject |

## 43. Security & Privacy

Speech data can contain highly sensitive information.

Checklist:

```text
[ ] audio/video assets private
[ ] signed URLs short-lived
[ ] transcript text not dumped to logs
[ ] PII redaction policy defined
[ ] raw provider result stored private
[ ] transcript access controlled by tenant/case
[ ] retention policy applies to raw audio and transcript
[ ] provider region/data residency reviewed
[ ] reviewer access audited
[ ] generated subtitle files treated as sensitive too
```

Important:

```text
Transcript can be more searchable and leak-prone than audio.
Protect transcript at least as strongly as source media.
```

---

## 44. Common Anti-Patterns

| Anti-pattern | Dampak | Alternatif |
|---|---|---|
| STT langsung di Jersey request | timeout/thread exhaustion | async job |
| Upload video ke STT tanpa audio check | wasted cost | ffprobe audio stream validation |
| Tidak normalize audio | provider error/poor accuracy | audio preparation step |
| Simpan transcript plain text saja | tidak bisa subtitle/review | segment + word model |
| Tidak simpan timestamps | search/playback lemah | word/segment timestamps |
| Tidak simpan provider job ID | recovery sulit | provider_job_id field |
| Worker block selama provider running | thread waste | submit + poll pattern |
| Retry provider submit tanpa idempotency | duplicate cost | deterministic job name/token |
| Log full transcript | PII leak | redacted logs |
| Diarization dianggap identity | privacy/security mistake | speaker label only |
| Semua audio di-convert WAV | storage/cost bloat | provider-compatible format strategy |
| No chunking for long audio | retry mahal | chunk + merge |
| No confidence/review route | poor transcript accepted | threshold + review |

---

## 46. Minimal Implementation untuk Tahap Awal

Implementasi awal yang realistis:

```text
1. transcription_policy table
2. audio metadata validation
3. FFmpeg audio extraction for video
4. normalized audio asset
5. SpeechToTextProvider interface
6. one provider implementation first
7. provider_job_id persistence
8. polling worker
9. transcript_result + transcript_segment tables
10. full-text search over transcript
11. audit events
12. basic low-confidence review routing
```

Mulai sederhana:

```text
Audio/video upload
→ validate
→ extract audio if video
→ submit provider batch job
→ poll
→ fetch result
→ persist full text + segments
→ searchable transcript
```

Jangan langsung menggabungkan diarization, subtitle, redaction, translation, dan summarization dalam versi pertama. Tambahkan bertahap.

## Response:
7/13/2026, 10:39:56 PM

Saya lanjut ke **Seri 12/20**: streaming transcription. Di sini fokusnya bukan batch file lagi, tetapi sesi real-time: audio chunks, partial/final transcript, latency, reconnect, backpressure, session state, dan bridge dari WebSocket client ke provider STT.

# Seri 12/20 - Streaming Transcription  
## WebSocket / Audio Chunks / Partial-Final Transcript / Latency Budget / Session State / Backpressure / Reconnect / Provider Bridge / Transcript Assembly

## 1. Introduction

Seri ini membahas **Streaming Speech-to-Text**, yaitu transkripsi audio secara real-time.

Bedanya dengan batch transcription:

```text id="oj9ni8"
Batch STT:
audio/video sudah jadi file
→ submit job
→ tunggu hasil akhir

Streaming STT:
audio dikirim sebagai chunks real-time
→ provider mengembalikan partial transcript
→ transcript final dibentuk bertahap
```

Streaming transcription cocok untuk:

```text id="74807w"
- live captioning
- call center assist
- meeting live notes
- voice command
- real-time compliance monitoring
- live interview transcription
- interactive voice application
```

Amazon Transcribe streaming menerima audio real-time dan mengembalikan transcript real-time, berbeda dari batch transcription yang memakai file media. Azure Speech-to-Text menyediakan real-time speech-to-text melalui Speech SDK, CLI, dan REST untuk short audio; Google Cloud Speech-to-Text juga menyediakan streaming recognition untuk audio seperti input mikrofon. ([AWS Documentation](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/transcribe/latest/dg/streaming.html?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

## 2. Mental Model

```text id="z1vrnr"
Streaming transcription = real-time session protocol

Bukan:
- upload file
- tunggu result
- single response

Tapi:
- open session
- stream audio chunks
- receive partial results
- stabilize final results
- handle reconnect
- manage latency/backpressure
- persist assembled transcript
```

Core problem-nya bukan hanya “menerima audio”, tapi menjaga:

```text id="umc8ij"
- session lifecycle
- chunk ordering
- audio format consistency
- provider connection
- partial/final transcript semantics
- latency budget
- retry/reconnect behavior
- transcript assembly
- security and privacy
```

---

## 3. Recommended Architecture

Untuk Java/Jersey-based microservice, REST/Jersey tetap cocok untuk control plane:

```text id="c34svp"
POST /streaming-transcription-sessions
GET  /streaming-transcription-sessions/{sessionId}
POST /streaming-transcription-sessions/{sessionId}/stop
GET  /streaming-transcription-sessions/{sessionId}/transcript
```

Tetapi audio stream sebaiknya lewat **WebSocket**, **HTTP/2 streaming**, atau service khusus. Jakarta WebSocket mendefinisikan API untuk server dan client endpoint WebSocket di Java, dan Jakarta EE tutorial menyebut WebSocket memungkinkan endpoint server/client dalam web application. ([Jakarta EE](https://jakarta.ee/specifications/websocket/?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

```text id="9tbpky"
Client
  │
  ├── REST: create streaming session
  │
  ▼
Jersey API
  │
  ├── create session row
  ├── authorize tenant/case
  ├── return websocket URL/token
  │
  ▼
Client
  │
  ├── WebSocket: send audio chunks
  │
  ▼
Streaming Gateway
  │
  ├── validate session token
  ├── receive chunks
  ├── apply backpressure
  ├── bridge to STT provider
  ├── receive partial/final transcript
  ├── publish transcript events
  └── persist final transcript
```

---

## 4. Diagram

```text id="u1ujhj"
Streaming Transcription Pipeline
│
├── 1. Session Control Plane
│   ├── create session
│   ├── authorize tenant/case
│   ├── configure language/provider
│   ├── issue stream token
│   └── stop/finalize session
│
├── 2. Audio Stream Plane
│   ├── WebSocket open
│   ├── audio format negotiation
│   ├── binary audio chunks
│   ├── sequence numbers
│   ├── timestamps
│   ├── heartbeat
│   └── close/finalize
│
├── 3. Provider Bridge
│   ├── AWS Transcribe Streaming
│   ├── Azure Speech SDK
│   ├── Google Streaming Recognition
│   ├── local/on-prem ASR
│   └── provider result adapter
│
├── 4. Transcript Event Stream
│   ├── partial transcript
│   ├── final transcript
│   ├── word timestamps
│   ├── speaker/channel info
│   ├── confidence
│   └── error/control events
│
├── 5. Transcript Assembly
│   ├── merge partial/final
│   ├── deduplicate revisions
│   ├── maintain segment order
│   ├── preserve offsets
│   └── emit stable transcript
│
└── 6. Persistence / Workflow
    ├── session record
    ├── transcript segments
    ├── events
    ├── recording artifact optional
    ├── review if needed
    └── final transcript result
```

---

## 5. Practical Skill Map

| Skill Area | Yang perlu dikuasai | Kenapa penting |
|---|---|---|
| Session lifecycle | create/open/active/closing/finalized/failed | Streaming adalah stateful |
| WebSocket protocol | binary chunks, text control messages, close codes | Audio real-time butuh bidirectional channel |
| Audio chunking | chunk duration, sequence, timestamp | Menjaga order dan latency |
| Audio format | PCM/WebM/Opus/sample rate/channel | Provider compatibility |
| Provider bridge | AWS/Azure/Google/local streaming adapter | Vendor abstraction |
| Partial vs final result | partial bisa berubah, final stabil | Transcript assembly benar |
| Backpressure | client terlalu cepat/provider lambat | Cegah memory bloat |
| Reconnect | jaringan mobile/browser tidak stabil | Session resilience |
| Heartbeat | detect stale connection | Cleanup dan recovery |
| Latency budget | capture → provider → client | Real-time UX |
| Transcript assembly | merge revisions, finalize segment | Output konsisten |
| Persistence | events + final transcript | Audit/search |
| Security | stream token, tenant/case auth, PII | Audio sangat sensitif |
| Observability | lag, dropped chunks, provider latency | Operability |

---

## 6. Session State Machine

```text id="un03ce"
CREATED
  │
  ▼
WAITING_FOR_CLIENT
  │
  ├── timeout ───────────► EXPIRED
  │
  ▼
CONNECTED
  │
  ▼
STREAMING
  │
  ├── client pause ──────► PAUSED
  │
  ├── disconnect ────────► DISCONNECTED
  │
  ├── stop requested ────► CLOSING
  │
  ├── provider error ────► FAILED
  │
  ▼
CLOSING
  │
  ▼
FINALIZING
  │
  ▼
COMPLETED

Optional:
CANCELLED
RECONNECTING
EXPIRED
```

State table:

| State | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `CREATED` | Session dibuat via REST |
| `WAITING_FOR_CLIENT` | WebSocket belum connect |
| `CONNECTED` | WebSocket connected, belum audio |
| `STREAMING` | Audio chunks sedang mengalir |
| `PAUSED` | Client pause, session masih valid |
| `DISCONNECTED` | Koneksi putus, masih bisa reconnect |
| `RECONNECTING` | Client mencoba resume |
| `CLOSING` | Stop requested, tidak menerima chunk baru |
| `FINALIZING` | Menunggu provider final result |
| `COMPLETED` | Transcript final tersedia |
| `FAILED` | Session gagal |
| `CANCELLED` | User/system cancel |
| `EXPIRED` | Session timeout |

---

## 11. WebSocket Message Protocol

Gunakan campuran:

```text id="ia65sl"
- text JSON messages for control
- binary messages for audio chunks
```

### Client → Server control message

```json id="rsvtky"
{
  "type": "START",
  "sessionId": "ses_123",
  "sequence": 0,
  "audio": {
    "encoding": "PCM_S16LE",
    "sampleRateHz": 16000,
    "channelCount": 1
  }
}
```

### Client → Server binary audio chunk

Binary payload:

```text id="elxob5"
raw PCM bytes / encoded audio frame
```

Associated metadata can be sent in separate control message or small binary header:

```json id="n5mgxc"
{
  "type": "AUDIO_CHUNK_META",
  "sequence": 42,
  "timestampMs": 840,
  "durationMs": 20,
  "byteLength": 640
}
```

Practical recommendation:

```text id="f3xoyj"
For browser/client simplicity:
- text START message
- binary chunks in order
- server assigns receive timestamp
- optional sequence header if client can provide it
```

### Server → Client partial transcript

```json id="2y16s2"
{
  "type": "PARTIAL_TRANSCRIPT",
  "sessionId": "ses_123",
  "eventIndex": 17,
  "startMs": 1200,
  "endMs": 2600,
  "text": "selamat pagi kita",
  "isFinal": false
}
```

### Server → Client final transcript

```json id="w4lolu"
{
  "type": "FINAL_TRANSCRIPT",
  "sessionId": "ses_123",
  "eventIndex": 18,
  "startMs": 1200,
  "endMs": 3100,
  "text": "Selamat pagi, kita mulai.",
  "confidence": 0.91,
  "isFinal": true
}
```

---

## 12. Partial vs Final Transcript

Ini konsep paling penting.

```text id="133go4"
Partial transcript:
- cepat
- belum stabil
- bisa berubah
- cocok untuk live display

Final transcript:
- lebih stabil
- boleh disimpan sebagai segment final
- dipakai untuk search/audit
```

Example:

```text id="rq59yt"
Partial 1: "selamat"
Partial 2: "selamat pagi"
Partial 3: "selamat pagi kita"
Final:     "Selamat pagi, kita mulai."
```

Jangan append semua partial ke final transcript. Kalau tidak, hasilnya jadi duplikat:

```text id="bbq1zn"
selamat selamat pagi selamat pagi kita Selamat pagi, kita mulai.
```

Correct model:

```text id="8c3kia"
display partial as temporary text
persist final as stable segment
persist partial only as event/debug if needed
```

---

## 13. Transcript Assembly

Assembly logic:

```text id="xrgo38"
1. Receive partial event
2. Update live buffer for current unstable span
3. Send partial to client UI
4. Do not commit final segment yet

5. Receive final event
6. Replace corresponding partial buffer
7. Commit final segment
8. Advance stable transcript cursor
```

Pseudo-code:

```java id="n4d0lh"
public final class StreamingTranscriptAssembler {

    private final Map<String, LivePartialBuffer> partialBuffers = new ConcurrentHashMap<>();
    private final List<TranscriptSegment> finalSegments = new CopyOnWriteArrayList<>();

    public AssemblyResult onEvent(ProviderTranscriptEvent event) {
        if (!event.isFinal()) {
            partialBuffers.put(event.resultId(), LivePartialBuffer.from(event));
            return AssemblyResult.partial(event.text());
        }

        partialBuffers.remove(event.resultId());

        TranscriptSegment segment = new TranscriptSegment(
                event.startMs(),
                event.endMs(),
                event.text(),
                event.confidence(),
                event.speakerLabel()
        );

        finalSegments.add(segment);

        return AssemblyResult.finalSegment(segment);
    }
}
```

Real provider events may not expose `resultId`; build a key from time range/provider alternative index if needed.

---

## 14. Latency Budget

Streaming transcription UX tergantung latency total:

```text id="eq8ovw"
microphone capture
+ client encoding
+ network upload
+ server buffering
+ provider processing
+ provider response
+ server fan-out
+ UI render
= end-to-end latency
```

Typical budget categories:

| Stage | Target thinking |
|---|---|
| audio chunk duration | 20-250 ms |
| client buffer | as small as stable |
| server queue | near zero |
| provider latency | dominant factor |
| partial display | fast |
| final stabilization | can be slower |
| network jitter | must tolerate |

Rule praktis:

```text id="7rvpi7"
Low latency requires small chunks,
but too-small chunks increase overhead.

Start with 100ms chunks,
then tune based on provider and client constraints.
```

---

## 15. Audio Chunking

Chunk size trade-off:

| Chunk Duration | Pros | Cons |
|---:|---|---|
| 20 ms | low latency, telephony-like | high overhead |
| 50 ms | good real-time | still many messages |
| 100 ms | balanced | common starting point |
| 250 ms | lower overhead | more latency |
| 1000 ms | simple | bad live UX |

Recommended starting point:

```text id="v1vgnt"
Browser/live caption: 100 ms
Telephony bridge: 20-50 ms
Low-cost/non-interactive live: 250 ms
```

For PCM 16kHz 16-bit mono:

```text id="tmkjg4"
16000 samples/sec
× 2 bytes/sample
× 0.1 sec
= 3200 bytes per 100 ms
```

---

## 16. Audio Format Strategy

Streaming provider often prefers raw PCM or specific encoded formats.

| Format | Pros | Cons |
|---|---|---|
| PCM S16LE | simple, provider-friendly | bandwidth high |
| Opus/WebM | efficient in browser | provider compatibility varies |
| FLAC | compressed lossless | harder for true low-latency stream |
| μ-law 8kHz | telephony compatible | lower quality |
| AAC | common media | streaming handling more complex |

Practical architecture:

```text id="u43ws6"
Browser sends WebM/Opus
  → server transcodes? expensive and adds latency

Browser sends PCM via Web Audio
  → more bandwidth
  → simpler provider bridge

Telephony sends μ-law
  → provider telephony mode if supported
```

For enterprise system, define supported streaming profiles:

```text id="b12fye"
PROFILE_BROWSER_PCM_16K_MONO
PROFILE_TELEPHONY_MULAW_8K
PROFILE_INTERNAL_PCM_16K_MONO
```

---

## 17. Provider Bridge Model

```java id="b4y7dw"
public interface StreamingSpeechProvider {

    StreamingProviderSession open(StreamingProviderRequest request);

    void sendAudio(StreamingProviderSession session, AudioChunk chunk);

    Publisher<ProviderTranscriptEvent> transcriptEvents(StreamingProviderSession session);

    void halfClose(StreamingProviderSession session);

    void close(StreamingProviderSession session);
}
```

Request:

```java id="at8syp"
public record StreamingProviderRequest(
        UUID sessionId,
        String languageCode,
        AudioFormatSpec audioFormat,
        boolean enablePartialResults,
        boolean enableWordTimestamps,
        boolean enableSpeakerDiarization,
        Map<String, String> vocabulary
) {}
```

Audio chunk:

```java id="58d7sx"
public record AudioChunk(
        long sequence,
        long timestampMs,
        byte[] bytes,
        boolean endOfStream
) {}
```

Provider event:

```java id="8a0izm"
public record ProviderTranscriptEvent(
        String providerName,
        String providerResultId,
        long startMs,
        long endMs,
        String text,
        Double confidence,
        String speakerLabel,
        boolean finalResult,
        Map<String, Object> raw
) {}
```

---

## 18. AWS Streaming Pattern

Amazon Transcribe streaming supports real-time transcription by sending media in real time and receiving transcript output in real time. AWS documentation also documents setup with HTTP/2 or WebSockets directly when not using an SDK. ([AWS Documentation](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/transcribe/latest/dg/streaming.html?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

Pattern:

```text id="t214cx"
Client WebSocket
  │
  ▼
Your Streaming Gateway
  │
  ▼
AWS Transcribe Streaming connection
  │
  ├── send audio events
  └── receive transcript events
```

Important design points:

```text id="gdrusy"
- do not expose cloud credentials to browser
- server signs/owns provider connection
- map provider events to canonical events
- handle provider limit/rate errors
```

---

## 19. Azure Streaming Pattern

Azure Speech-to-Text supports real-time transcription via Speech SDK, Speech CLI, and short-audio REST API; Azure Speech documentation positions speech-to-text as real-time and batch transcription of audio streams into text. ([Microsoft Learn](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/ai-services/speech-service/speech-to-text?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

Typical Java pattern:

```text id="uqw5zo"
Your Streaming Gateway
  │
  ├── receive audio chunks
  ├── push chunks into Azure SDK audio stream
  ├── subscribe to recognizing events
  ├── subscribe to recognized events
  └── map to partial/final transcript
```

Conceptual mapping:

| Azure concept | Internal event |
|---|---|
| recognizing | `PARTIAL_TRANSCRIPT` |
| recognized | `FINAL_TRANSCRIPT` |
| canceled | `PROVIDER_ERROR` |
| session started/stopped | session lifecycle event |

---

## 20. Google Streaming Pattern

Google Cloud Speech-to-Text provides streaming recognition examples for audio such as microphone input. Google docs describe streaming speech recognition as streaming audio data to Speech-to-Text and receiving streaming recognition responses. ([Google Cloud Documentation](https://docs.cloud.google.com/speech-to-text/docs/streaming-recognize?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

Typical pattern:

```text id="7h2a0u"
Your Streaming Gateway
  │
  ├── open Google streaming recognition call
  ├── send config first
  ├── send audio chunks
  ├── receive streaming responses
  └── map interim/final results
```

Design caution:

```text id="oahr4n"
Google/AWS/Azure event semantics are not identical.
Normalize to canonical PARTIAL/FINAL event model.
```

---

## 21. WebSocket Gateway Skeleton

```java id="1g3ojl"
@ServerEndpoint("/stt/stream/{sessionId}")
public class StreamingTranscriptionEndpoint {

    private static StreamingTranscriptionService streamingService;

    @OnOpen
    public void onOpen(
            Session websocketSession,
            @PathParam("sessionId") String sessionId,
            EndpointConfig config
    ) {
        StreamingConnection connection = streamingService.openConnection(
                UUID.fromString(sessionId),
                websocketSession
        );

        websocketSession.getUserProperties().put("connection", connection);
    }

    @OnMessage
    public void onTextMessage(String message, Session websocketSession) {
        StreamingConnection connection =
                (StreamingConnection) websocketSession.getUserProperties().get("connection");

        streamingService.handleControlMessage(connection, message);
    }

    @OnMessage
    public void onBinaryMessage(ByteBuffer audioBytes, Session websocketSession) {
        StreamingConnection connection =
                (StreamingConnection) websocketSession.getUserProperties().get("connection");

        streamingService.handleAudioChunk(connection, audioBytes);
    }

    @OnClose
    public void onClose(Session websocketSession, CloseReason closeReason) {
        StreamingConnection connection =
                (StreamingConnection) websocketSession.getUserProperties().get("connection");

        streamingService.closeConnection(connection, closeReason);
    }

    @OnError
    public void onError(Session websocketSession, Throwable error) {
        StreamingConnection connection =
                (StreamingConnection) websocketSession.getUserProperties().get("connection");

        streamingService.handleError(connection, error);
    }
}
```

Catatan:

```text id="9dyhmt"
Dependency injection di @ServerEndpoint bisa berbeda tergantung runtime.
Untuk production, pastikan lifecycle dan DI integration jelas.
```

---

## 22. Streaming Service Skeleton

```java id="hmsgpz"
public final class StreamingTranscriptionService {

    private final StreamingSessionRepository sessionRepository;
    private final StreamingSpeechProviderRegistry providerRegistry;
    private final TranscriptEventRepository eventRepository;
    private final StreamingTranscriptAssembler assembler;

    public StreamingConnection openConnection(UUID sessionId, Session websocketSession) {
        StreamingSession session = sessionRepository.get(sessionId);

        validateCanConnect(session);

        StreamingSpeechProvider provider = providerRegistry.resolve(session);

        StreamingProviderSession providerSession = provider.open(
                session.toProviderRequest()
        );

        StreamingConnection connection = new StreamingConnection(
                session,
                websocketSession,
                provider,
                providerSession
        );

        sessionRepository.markConnected(sessionId, websocketSession.getId());

        provider.transcriptEvents(providerSession)
                .subscribe(event -> handleProviderEvent(connection, event));

        return connection;
    }

    public void handleAudioChunk(StreamingConnection connection, ByteBuffer audioBytes) {
        AudioChunk chunk = connection.nextChunk(audioBytes);

        connection.provider().sendAudio(connection.providerSession(), chunk);

        sessionRepository.updateLastChunk(
                connection.sessionId(),
                chunk.sequence(),
                chunk.timestampMs()
        );
    }

    private void handleProviderEvent(
            StreamingConnection connection,
            ProviderTranscriptEvent providerEvent
    ) {
        StreamingTranscriptEvent event = StreamingTranscriptEvent.from(providerEvent);

        eventRepository.insert(event);

        AssemblyResult assembly = assembler.onEvent(providerEvent);

        connection.sendToClient(assembly.toClientMessage());

        if (providerEvent.finalResult()) {
            eventRepository.insertFinalSegment(connection.sessionId(), assembly.segment());
        }
    }
}
```

---

## 23. Backpressure

Backpressure menangani kondisi:

```text id="5enzzt"
client sends faster than server/provider can process
```

Tanpa backpressure:

```text id="8avm0z"
WebSocket receives chunks
→ queue grows
→ memory grows
→ JVM GC pressure
→ latency spikes
→ pod dies
```

Strategies:

| Strategy | Description |
|---|---|
| bounded queue | cap audio chunks per session |
| drop policy | drop oldest/newest only if acceptable |
| pause signal | tell client to slow down |
| close connection | if client violates limits |
| provider flow control | respect SDK/HTTP2 backpressure |
| per-tenant/session quota | isolate noisy users |

Server → Client backpressure message:

```json id="03kd16"
{
  "type": "BACKPRESSURE",
  "reason": "SERVER_QUEUE_HIGH",
  "recommendedPauseMs": 200
}
```

Hard limits:

```text id="fnkbsq"
max queued audio duration per session: 2-5 seconds
max session duration: policy-based
max concurrent sessions per tenant
max reconnect attempts
```

---

## 24. Reconnect / Resume

Streaming reconnect is difficult because provider sessions often cannot resume exactly.

Options:

| Mode | Behavior |
|---|---|
| strict no-resume | disconnect ends session |
| soft resume | reconnect to same internal session but new provider session |
| client replay buffer | client resends last N seconds |
| server audio buffer | server keeps last N seconds |
| final-only persistence | partials may be lost, finals preserved |

Recommended baseline:

```text id="r3cqmh"
- final transcript segments are durable
- partial transcript may be lost
- client can reconnect within grace period
- client sends last acknowledged sequence
- server starts new provider stream if needed
```

Reconnect message:

```json id="qaq5tn"
{
  "type": "RESUME",
  "sessionId": "ses_123",
  "lastClientSequence": 1842,
  "lastReceivedServerEventIndex": 91
}
```

Server response:

```json id="aeypld"
{
  "type": "RESUME_ACCEPTED",
  "sessionId": "ses_123",
  "resumeMode": "NEW_PROVIDER_STREAM",
  "lastFinalSegmentIndex": 17
}
```

---

## 25. Heartbeat and Timeout

Heartbeats detect dead connections.

Client → Server:

```json id="wf1hd4"
{
  "type": "PING",
  "sequence": 1201,
  "clientTimeMs": 421000
}
```

Server → Client:

```json id="5uz0jb"
{
  "type": "PONG",
  "serverTime": "2026-07-13T19:05:00+07:00"
}
```

Timeout rules:

```text id="2nudv8"
- no heartbeat for 30s → mark connection stale
- no audio for N seconds → PAUSED or idle timeout
- no reconnect within grace period → FINALIZING / FAILED
- provider no response for threshold → provider timeout
```

---

## 26. Session Token Security

Do not rely only on session ID.

```text id="v749or"
streamUrl = wss://.../stt/stream/{sessionId}
streamToken = short-lived signed token
```

Token should contain or map to:

```text id="sohprr"
- sessionId
- tenantId
- userId
- expiry
- allowed audio format
- permissions
```

Validation:

```text id="2z8yc0"
on WebSocket open:
- verify token
- verify session status
- verify tenant/case access
- verify token not expired
- verify one active connection policy
```

Never expose cloud provider credentials directly to browser/mobile client.

---

## 27. Recording Strategy

Question: should the system persist raw streamed audio?

Options:

| Option | Meaning | Pros | Cons |
|---|---|---|---|
| no recording | only transcript persisted | privacy/storage lower | cannot audit audio |
| rolling buffer only | temporary audio buffer | reconnect/debug | limited audit |
| full recording | store audio chunks/artifact | audit/reprocessing | privacy/storage/cost |
| consent-based recording | configurable per case | defensible | more policy complexity |

If storing recording:

```text id="x0mebh"
- make it explicit
- require consent/legal basis
- store as media_asset
- apply retention
- encrypt
- audit access
```

---

## 28. Transcript Persistence Strategy

Persist:

```text id="nvexxn"
- final transcript segments
- session metadata
- provider metadata
- optional partial events
- optional audio recording asset
```

Do not necessarily persist every partial event forever. Partial events can be high-volume.

Policy:

| Event Type | Retention |
|---|---|
| final segment | long enough for business case |
| partial event | short debug TTL or not persisted |
| provider raw event | optional short TTL |
| audio chunks | only if recording enabled |
| audit events | per compliance policy |

---

## 29. Provider Event Normalization

Canonical mapping:

| Provider concept | Internal |
|---|---|
| interim / recognizing / partial | `PARTIAL_TRANSCRIPT` |
| final / recognized | `FINAL_TRANSCRIPT` |
| word time offset | `WORD_RESULT` |
| error/cancelled | `PROVIDER_ERROR` |
| session start/stop | lifecycle event |

Store raw event only when needed:

```json id="n0y08x"
{
  "eventType": "FINAL_TRANSCRIPT",
  "providerName": "AZURE_SPEECH",
  "startMs": 1200,
  "endMs": 3100,
  "text": "Selamat pagi, kita mulai.",
  "confidence": 0.91,
  "raw": {
    "providerSpecific": "..."
  }
}
```

---

## 30. Failure Classification

| Failure | Meaning | Retry/Action |
|---|---|---|
| `SESSION_TOKEN_INVALID` | bad/expired stream token | reject connection |
| `SESSION_ALREADY_ACTIVE` | duplicate connection not allowed | reject or replace |
| `AUDIO_FORMAT_MISMATCH` | client sends wrong format | close with error |
| `AUDIO_SEQUENCE_GAP` | missing chunks | warn/reconnect/review |
| `BACKPRESSURE_LIMIT_EXCEEDED` | queue too high | throttle/close |
| `PROVIDER_STREAM_FAILED` | provider connection failed | reconnect provider if possible |
| `PROVIDER_RATE_LIMIT` | quota exceeded | fail/queue/retry |
| `CLIENT_DISCONNECTED` | network closed | wait reconnect grace |
| `RECONNECT_WINDOW_EXPIRED` | no resume | finalize/fail |
| `EMPTY_STREAM` | no audio received | cancel/complete empty |
| `LOW_CONFIDENCE_STREAM` | result poor | review |
| `MAX_SESSION_DURATION_EXCEEDED` | policy limit | stop/finalize |
| `PERSISTENCE_FAILED` | DB/object storage issue | retry service layer |

---

## 31. WebSocket Close Codes / Error Messages

Use structured close messages before closing when possible.

Server → Client:

```json id="ja3k2k"
{
  "type": "ERROR",
  "errorClass": "AUDIO_FORMAT_MISMATCH",
  "message": "Expected PCM_S16LE 16000Hz mono."
}
```

Close scenarios:

| Scenario | Action |
|---|---|
| invalid token | close immediately |
| unsupported format | close after error |
| quota exceeded | close after error |
| provider unavailable | send error, mark failed |
| client idle | close after timeout |
| normal stop | finalizing then normal close |

---

## 32. Transcript Assembly Persistence

Recommended pattern:

```text id="kzy9w8"
partial event
→ memory/live buffer
→ optional short-term event table

final event
→ event table
→ final segment table
→ search index update
→ client notification
```

Search index should update from final segments, not partials.

```sql id="1jzxa9"
ALTER TABLE streaming_transcript_segment
ADD COLUMN search_vector tsvector;

CREATE INDEX idx_streaming_segment_search
ON streaming_transcript_segment USING GIN (search_vector);
```

---

## 33. Client UI Behavior

Client should treat transcript as two layers:

```text id="a5sb9l"
Stable transcript:
- final segments
- not expected to change

Live transcript:
- current partial
- may be replaced
```

UI model:

```text id="g1t85e"
[Final]  Selamat pagi, kita mulai.
[Final]  Agenda hari ini adalah review dokumen.
[Live]   kemudian kita akan membahas...
```

Do not store partial text in client as permanent transcript.

---

## 34. Latency Metrics

Measure these separately:

| Metric | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `stream_audio_chunk_received_total` | input volume |
| `stream_audio_queue_depth` | backpressure signal |
| `stream_audio_queue_duration_ms` | queued audio duration |
| `stream_provider_send_latency_ms` | server → provider |
| `stream_provider_response_latency_ms` | provider event delay |
| `stream_partial_latency_ms` | audio timestamp → partial |
| `stream_final_latency_ms` | audio timestamp → final |
| `stream_reconnect_total` | network stability |
| `stream_dropped_chunk_total` | quality risk |
| `stream_session_duration_seconds` | usage/cost |
| `stream_concurrent_sessions` | capacity |
| `stream_provider_error_total` | reliability |
| `stream_backpressure_total` | overload |

---

## 35. Observability Dashboard

Useful dashboard:

```text id="y22g7a"
Streaming STT Dashboard
├── active sessions
├── concurrent sessions by tenant
├── average partial latency
├── average final latency
├── provider error rate
├── reconnect rate
├── dropped chunk count
├── backpressure events
├── session duration distribution
├── low confidence sessions
└── cost estimate by audio duration
```

Log context:

```text id="j6ri27"
correlationId
tenantId
caseId
sessionId
connectionId
provider
sequence
audioTimestampMs
eventIndex
latencyMs
failureClass
```

Do not log raw transcript/audio unless explicitly allowed and redacted.

## 36. Audit Events

```text id="c3gwxg"
STREAM_SESSION_CREATED
STREAM_CLIENT_CONNECTED
STREAM_STARTED
STREAM_AUDIO_FORMAT_NEGOTIATED
STREAM_PROVIDER_CONNECTED
STREAM_PARTIAL_TRANSCRIPT_RECEIVED
STREAM_FINAL_TRANSCRIPT_RECEIVED
STREAM_CLIENT_DISCONNECTED
STREAM_CLIENT_RECONNECTED
STREAM_BACKPRESSURE_SIGNALLED
STREAM_STOP_REQUESTED
STREAM_FINALIZED
STREAM_FAILED
STREAM_CANCELLED
```

Example:

```json id="9788cf"
{
  "eventType": "STREAM_FINAL_TRANSCRIPT_RECEIVED",
  "sessionId": "ses_123",
  "metadata": {
    "provider": "AWS_TRANSCRIBE_STREAMING",
    "startMs": 1200,
    "endMs": 3100,
    "confidence": 0.91,

  }
```

## 37. Security and Privacy

Streaming audio is highly sensitive because it can include live personal data.

Checklist:

```text id="09quze"
[ ] WebSocket requires short-lived stream token
[ ] REST session creation checks tenant/case permission
[ ] one active connection policy defined
[ ] audio format and session options locked
[ ] transcript not logged raw
[ ] audio recording is opt-in/policy-controlled
[ ] provider region/data residency reviewed
[ ] signed URLs short-lived if recording is saved
[ ] retention policy applies to transcript/audio/events
[ ] reviewer/admin access audited
```

Important:

```text id="t6hvmd"
Partial transcript can contain sensitive text too.
Treat partials as sensitive even if not persisted long-term.
```

---

## 38. Kubernetes / Scaling Model

Streaming sessions are long-lived connections, so scaling differs from REST.

```text id="uoe6en"
REST API pod:
- short-lived requests
- easy horizontal scaling

Streaming gateway pod:
- long-lived WebSocket connections
- connection state
- provider sessions
- backpressure queues
```

Recommendations:

```text id="oc86bv"
- separate deployment for streaming gateway
- sticky session if in-memory state
- externalize state if reconnect across pods
- limit sessions per pod
- graceful shutdown drains sessions
- autoscale by active sessions and CPU/network
```

Graceful shutdown:

```text id="7892s3"
pod receives SIGTERM
→ stop accepting new streams
→ notify clients to reconnect
→ finalize/close provider sessions
→ flush final segments
→ terminate
```

---

## 39. Local / On-Prem Streaming ASR

If using local ASR model:

```text id="c29de4"
Streaming Gateway
  │
  ▼
ASR Inference Service
  │
  ├── GPU/CPU model
  ├── per-session audio buffer
  ├── streaming decoder
  ├── partial/final output
  └── model-level batching
```

Considerations:

```text id="yrt3si"
- GPU scheduling
- max concurrent streams
- model warmup
- per-session memory
- latency vs accuracy tradeoff
- language model customization
```

Do not run heavy model inference inside Jersey API.

---

## 40. Common Anti-Patterns

| Anti-pattern | Dampak | Alternatif |
|---|---|---|
| Treat streaming like file upload | wrong architecture | session protocol |
| Append every partial to transcript | duplicate transcript | partial buffer + final commit |
| No sequence numbers | hard to detect gaps | sequence/timestamps |
| No backpressure | memory blowup | bounded queue/throttle |
| WebSocket token long-lived | stream hijack risk | short-lived stream token |
| Browser talks directly to cloud provider with credentials | credential leak | server-side provider bridge |
| No reconnect strategy | bad mobile UX | resume window |
| Store huge audio chunks in DB | DB bloat | object storage or no recording |
| No latency metrics | UX impossible to tune | partial/final latency metrics |
| Run streaming on same pods as REST | unstable API latency | separate streaming gateway |
| No graceful shutdown | lost sessions | drain/reconnect |
| Persist partials forever | storage/PII risk | short TTL or no persistence |

---

## 41. Senior Engineer Checklist

```text id="br5qmc"
Architecture
[ ] REST control plane separate from streaming plane
[ ] WebSocket/streaming gateway exists
[ ] provider bridge abstraction exists
[ ] long-lived sessions not handled like normal REST
[ ] streaming gateway scaled separately

Session
[ ] session state machine defined
[ ] stream token short-lived
[ ] heartbeat timeout defined
[ ] reconnect window defined
[ ] max session duration defined

Audio
[ ] audio format negotiated
[ ] sample rate/channel/encoding locked
[ ] chunk duration defined
[ ] sequence/timestamp handled
[ ] backpressure policy exists

Transcript
[ ] partial vs final semantics clear
[ ] partials not appended permanently
[ ] final segments persisted
[ ] search index uses final segments
[ ] provider events normalized

Failure
[ ] client disconnect handling
[ ] provider failure handling
[ ] reconnect handling
[ ] sequence gap detection
[ ] quota/rate limit handling

Security/Ops
[ ] no raw transcript logs
[ ] recording policy explicit
[ ] tenant/case auth enforced
[ ] active session metrics
[ ] partial/final latency metrics
[ ] graceful shutdown strategy
```

---

## 42. Minimal Implementation untuk Tahap Awal

Implementasi awal yang realistis:

```text id="fjdyzs"
1. REST API to create streaming session
2. short-lived stream token
3. Jakarta WebSocket endpoint
4. binary audio chunk handling
5. one audio profile: PCM 16kHz mono
6. provider bridge interface
7. one provider implementation first
8. partial/final event normalization
9. transcript assembler
10. final segment persistence
11. heartbeat and idle timeout
12. basic backpressure with bounded queue
```

Jangan langsung membangun:

```text id="lh9olo"
- multi-provider fallback
- diarization
- translation
- recording
- live summarization
- speaker identification
```

Tambahkan setelah core streaming semantics stabil.

---

## 43. Roadmap Singkat Setelah Seri 12

Urutan berikutnya:

```text id="e3mu9l"
Seri 13 → Speaker Diarization, Timestamp Alignment, Subtitle SRT/VTT
Seri 14 → Text-to-Speech
Seri 15 → FFmpeg Media Processing
Seri 16 → Face Detection vs Face Verification vs Face Recognition
Seri 17 → Face ID, Passkey, WebAuthn, dan biometric boundary
```

Streaming transcription adalah masalah **stateful real-time protocol**, bukan sekadar speech-to-text API. Kunci desainnya: partial/final semantics benar, session lifecycle kuat, backpressure ada, reconnect jelas, dan provider result dinormalisasi.

# Seri 13/20 - Speaker Diarization, Timestamp Alignment, Subtitle SRT/VTT  
## Speaker Labels, Word/Segment Timing, Subtitle Generation, Overlap Handling, Correction, dan Review Workflow

## 1. Introduction

Di seri 11 dan 12, kita membahas **batch transcription** dan **streaming transcription**. Seri ini membahas lapisan berikutnya: bagaimana transcript dibuat menjadi **time-aligned, speaker-aware, reviewable, searchable, dan exportable**.

Ada tiga konsep utama:

```text id="p8a2wj"
1. Speaker diarization
   → memisahkan “siapa bicara kapan”
   → output: SPEAKER_1, SPEAKER_2, dst.

2. Timestamp alignment
   → memberi start/end time pada word, segment, speaker turn, subtitle cue

3. Subtitle generation
   → mengubah transcript + timestamp menjadi SRT/VTT
```

Amazon Transcribe mendukung speaker diarization untuk batch transcription dan real-time streaming; Google Speech-to-Text mendukung word time offsets yang memberi waktu mulai dan akhir untuk kata yang dikenali; WebVTT adalah format cue berbasis time interval untuk text track; SRT adalah format teks subtitle berbasis timing yang tidak menyimpan audio/video itu sendiri. ([AWS Documentation](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/transcribe/latest/dg/diarization.html?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

## 2. Mental Model

```text id="8ym0wr"
Raw transcript:
"Selamat pagi kita mulai meeting. Baik saya mulai dari agenda pertama."

Speaker-aware transcript:
[SPEAKER_1 00:01.200 - 00:04.600] Selamat pagi kita mulai meeting.
[SPEAKER_2 00:05.000 - 00:08.700] Baik saya mulai dari agenda pertama.

Subtitle:
1
00:00:01,200 --> 00:00:04,600
Selamat pagi kita mulai meeting.

2
00:00:05,000 --> 00:00:08,700
Baik saya mulai dari agenda pertama.
```

Yang sering salah dipahami:

```text id="4f8zh3"
Diarization ≠ speaker identification.

Diarization:
- SPEAKER_1
- SPEAKER_2
- tidak tahu nama orang

Speaker identification:
- Budi
- Sari
- Agent A
- Customer B
- butuh voice identity / enrollment / channel mapping / metadata
```

Untuk sistem enterprise, simpan `speakerLabel` sebagai label teknis, bukan identitas orang, kecuali ada proses eksplisit untuk mapping identitas.

---

## 3. Diagram

```text id="7u1s8p"
Audio / Video
   │
   ▼
Speech-to-Text Provider
   │
   ├── raw transcript
   ├── word timestamps
   ├── speaker labels
   ├── channel labels
   └── confidence
   │
   ▼
Transcript Normalizer
   │
   ├── canonical words
   ├── canonical segments
   ├── speaker turns
   ├── timing correction
   └── confidence aggregation
   │
   ▼
Timestamp Alignment
   │
   ├── word → segment
   ├── segment → subtitle cue
   ├── chunk timestamp offset
   ├── overlap deduplication
   └── gap/silence handling
   │
   ▼
Outputs
   ├── searchable transcript
   ├── speaker-aware transcript
   ├── SRT
   ├── VTT
   ├── review payload
   └── audit trail
```

---

## 4. Practical Skill Map

| Area | Yang perlu dikuasai | Kenapa penting |
|---|---|---|
| Speaker diarization | Memisahkan speaker turn | Meeting/call transcript butuh “who spoke when” |
| Channel identification | Mapping channel audio ke role | Call center stereo lebih stabil daripada diarization |
| Speaker labeling | `SPEAKER_1`, `SPEAKER_2` | Label teknis, bukan identitas |
| Speaker identity mapping | Map label ke nama/role | Butuh metadata/review/consent |
| Word timestamps | start/end per word | Search, highlight, subtitle, playback |
| Segment timestamps | start/end per utterance | Review dan subtitle |
| Timestamp offset | chunk/local time → global time | Penting untuk chunked audio |
| Overlap handling | overlapping speech/cues | Subtitle dan transcript tidak kacau |
| Subtitle cue generation | SRT/VTT cue | Export ke player/video |
| Cue line breaking | max duration, max chars, readable text | UX subtitle |
| Transcript correction | edit text/speaker/timing | Human review |
| Alignment audit | provider, offset, correction history | Defensibility |
| Search/playback integration | click search result → play audio at timestamp | Case/evidence workflow |

---

## 5. Diarization vs Channel Identification vs Speaker Identification

| Concept | Pertanyaan | Output | Risiko |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Speaker diarization** | “Ada berapa pembicara dan kapan masing-masing bicara?” | `SPEAKER_1`, `SPEAKER_2` | Sedang |
| **Channel identification** | “Audio channel ini milik siapa/role apa?” | `CHANNEL_0 = agent`, `CHANNEL_1 = customer` | Lebih rendah jika channel metadata valid |
| **Speaker identification** | “Pembicara ini orang siapa?” | `Budi`, `Sari` | Tinggi, biometric/identity-sensitive |

Contoh:

```text id="b4y0x5"
Call center stereo:
- left channel  = agent
- right channel = customer

Meeting mono:
- diarization needed
- output speaker labels only

Voice identity:
- requires enrolled speaker profile
- privacy/compliance risk much higher
```

AWS menyebut speaker diarization sebagai partitioning speakers dalam audio untuk batch transcription atau real-time stream, bukan identifikasi nama orang. ([AWS Documentation](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/transcribe/latest/dg/diarization.html?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

## 6. Canonical Transcript Model

Transcript sebaiknya dinormalisasi menjadi beberapa level.

```text id="3bgj7y"
TranscriptionResult
├── fullText
├── speakers
├── segments
├── words
├── subtitles
└── corrections
```

### Speaker

```java id="f6zlee"
public record TranscriptSpeaker(
        String speakerLabel,
        String displayName,
        String role,
        Double confidence,
        boolean manuallyAssigned
) {}
```

### Segment

```java id="7wil5o"
public record TranscriptSegment(
        UUID id,
        int segmentIndex,
        String speakerLabel,
        String channelLabel,
        long startMs,
        long endMs,
        String text,
        Double confidence,
        List<TranscriptWord> words
) {}
```

### Word

```java id="3o2k3z"
public record TranscriptWord(
        UUID id,
        int wordIndex,
        String speakerLabel,
        long startMs,
        long endMs,
        String text,
        Double confidence
) {}
```

### Subtitle Cue

```java id="07yly4"
public record SubtitleCue(
        int cueIndex,
        long startMs,
        long endMs,
        String text,
        String speakerLabel,
        SubtitleFormat format
) {}

public enum SubtitleFormat {
    SRT,
    WEBVTT
}
```

## 7. Database Schema - Speakers

```sql id="cmasyz"
CREATE TABLE transcript_speaker (
    id                         UUID PRIMARY KEY,
    transcription_result_id     UUID NOT NULL REFERENCES transcription_result(id),

    speaker_label               VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL,
    display_name                TEXT,
    role_name                   VARCHAR(100),

    confidence                  NUMERIC(6,5),
    manually_assigned           BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT FALSE,
    assigned_by                 UUID,
    assigned_at                 TIMESTAMPTZ,

    metadata_json               JSONB,
    created_at                  TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),

    UNIQUE (transcription_result_id, speaker_label)
);
```

Examples:

```text id="h6j7zd"
speaker_label = SPEAKER_1
display_name  = null initially
role_name     = Agent / Customer / Host / Participant after review
```

## 8. Database Schema - Segments and Words

```sql id="utko8e"
CREATE TABLE transcript_segment (
    id                         UUID PRIMARY KEY,
    transcription_result_id     UUID NOT NULL REFERENCES transcription_result(id),

    segment_index               INTEGER NOT NULL,
    speaker_label               VARCHAR(100),
    channel_label               VARCHAR(100),

    start_ms                    BIGINT NOT NULL,
    end_ms                      BIGINT NOT NULL,

    text                        TEXT NOT NULL,
    confidence                  NUMERIC(6,5),

    source_provider             VARCHAR(100),
    source_event_id             TEXT,
    metadata_json               JSONB,

    created_at                  TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),

    UNIQUE (transcription_result_id, segment_index)
);
```

```sql id="x8wb7f"
CREATE TABLE transcript_word (
    id                         UUID PRIMARY KEY,
    transcription_result_id     UUID NOT NULL REFERENCES transcription_result(id),
    segment_id                  UUID REFERENCES transcript_segment(id),

    word_index                  INTEGER NOT NULL,
    speaker_label               VARCHAR(100),

    start_ms                    BIGINT,
    end_ms                      BIGINT,

    text                        TEXT NOT NULL,
    confidence                  NUMERIC(6,5),

    created_at                  TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),

    UNIQUE (transcription_result_id, word_index)
);
```

Index untuk playback/search:

```sql id="yjhc9x"
CREATE INDEX idx_transcript_segment_time
ON transcript_segment (transcription_result_id, start_ms, end_ms);

CREATE INDEX idx_transcript_segment_speaker
ON transcript_segment (transcription_result_id, speaker_label);

CREATE INDEX idx_transcript_word_time
ON transcript_word (transcription_result_id, start_ms, end_ms);
```

---

## 9. Timestamp Model

Gunakan **milliseconds from media start** sebagai canonical internal time.

```text id="ochkrn"
startMs = 1200
endMs   = 4600
```

Jangan simpan hanya string subtitle seperti:

```text id="uy671w"
00:00:01,200 --> 00:00:04,600
```

Simpan numeric timestamp untuk:

```text id="ushqad"
- query range
- playback seek
- subtitle export
- correction
- alignment
- chunk merging
```

Subtitle string bisa digenerate dari `startMs` dan `endMs`.

---

## 10. Word Timestamp Alignment

Google Speech-to-Text word time offsets menunjukkan waktu mulai dan akhir setiap kata yang dikenali, dihitung dari awal audio. ([Google Cloud Documentation](https://docs.cloud.google.com/speech-to-text/docs/v1/async-time-offsets?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

Canonical word:

```json id="pdzsyj"
{
  "wordIndex": 42,
  "text": "pembayaran",
  "startMs": 84200,
  "endMs": 84780,
  "confidence": 0.91,
  "speakerLabel": "SPEAKER_2"
}
```

Kalau provider hanya memberi segment timing, bukan word timing:

```text id="zywhf0"
Option 1: store segment timing only
Option 2: approximate word timing proportionally
Option 3: run forced alignment tool
```

Approximation harus ditandai:

```json id="j7saz0"
{
  "text": "pembayaran",
  "startMs": 84200,
  "endMs": 84780,
  "timingSource": "APPROXIMATED"
}
```

Jangan presentasikan timing approximate sebagai provider-certified timing.

---

## 11. Segment Construction

Provider bisa mengembalikan:

```text id="dgd23o"
- utterance segments
- alternatives
- words only
- speaker turns
- partial/final streaming events
```

Jika Anda punya words dengan timestamps, segment bisa dibentuk dengan heuristic:

```text id="o6aimr"
new segment when:
- speaker changes
- pause > threshold
- sentence boundary detected
- segment duration exceeds max
- text length exceeds max
```

Example thresholds:

```text id="328gqk"
pause threshold: 800-1500 ms
max segment duration: 8-12 seconds
max segment chars: 120-180 chars
```

Segment builder skeleton:

```java id="w2l15t"
public final class TranscriptSegmentBuilder {

    public List<TranscriptSegment> buildSegments(List<TranscriptWord> words) {
        List<TranscriptSegment> segments = new ArrayList<>();
        List<TranscriptWord> current = new ArrayList<>();

        for (TranscriptWord word : words) {
            if (current.isEmpty()) {
                current.add(word);
                continue;
            }

            TranscriptWord previous = current.get(current.size() - 1);

            boolean speakerChanged = !Objects.equals(
                    previous.speakerLabel(),
                    word.speakerLabel()
            );

            boolean longPause = word.startMs() - previous.endMs() > 1000;

            boolean tooLong = word.endMs() - current.get(0).startMs() > 10000;

            if (speakerChanged || longPause || tooLong) {
                segments.add(toSegment(segments.size(), current));
                current = new ArrayList<>();
            }

            current.add(word);
        }

        if (!current.isEmpty()) {
            segments.add(toSegment(segments.size(), current));
        }

        return segments;
    }

    private TranscriptSegment toSegment(int index, List<TranscriptWord> words) {
        return new TranscriptSegment(
                UUID.randomUUID(),
                index,
                words.get(0).speakerLabel(),
                null,
                words.get(0).startMs(),
                words.get(words.size() - 1).endMs(),
                words.stream().map(TranscriptWord::text).collect(Collectors.joining(" ")),
                meanConfidence(words),
                words
        );
    }
}
```

---

## 12. Speaker Turn Model

Speaker turn adalah continuous speaking interval oleh speaker yang sama.

```text id="8p9743"
SPEAKER_1 00:01.200 - 00:04.600
SPEAKER_2 00:05.000 - 00:08.700
SPEAKER_1 00:09.200 - 00:12.300
```

Database:

```sql id="mbqofu"
CREATE TABLE transcript_speaker_turn (
    id                         UUID PRIMARY KEY,
    transcription_result_id     UUID NOT NULL REFERENCES transcription_result(id),

    turn_index                  INTEGER NOT NULL,
    speaker_label               VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL,

    start_ms                    BIGINT NOT NULL,
    end_ms                      BIGINT NOT NULL,

    segment_ids                 UUID[],
    confidence                  NUMERIC(6,5),

    created_at                  TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),

    UNIQUE (transcription_result_id, turn_index)
);
```

Speaker turn berguna untuk:

```text id="50kube"
- diarization review
- speaker timeline visualization
- talk time analytics
- meeting/call navigation
```

---

## 13. Chunk Timestamp Offset

Kalau batch audio panjang dipecah menjadi chunks:

```text id="20dy4o"
chunk 0: 00:00-15:00
chunk 1: 15:00-30:00
```

Provider mungkin mengembalikan timestamp lokal chunk:

```text id="65dp3j"
chunk 1 result:
start = 00:02.000
end   = 00:05.000
```

Harus dikonversi ke global media time:

```text id="ryquks"
globalStart = chunkStartMs + localStartMs
            = 900000 + 2000
            = 902000 ms
```

Code:

```java id="m9rggs"
public TranscriptWord applyChunkOffset(
        TranscriptWord word,
        long chunkStartMs
) {
    return new TranscriptWord(
            word.id(),
            word.wordIndex(),
            word.speakerLabel(),
            word.startMs() + chunkStartMs,
            word.endMs() + chunkStartMs,
            word.text(),
            word.confidence()
    );
}
```

---

## 14. Overlap Handling

Chunk overlap membantu mencegah kata terpotong di boundary.

```text id="rxtqfh"
chunk 0: 00:00-15:05
chunk 1: 15:00-30:05
overlap: 5 seconds
```

Masalah:

```text id="o1j3z7"
words between 15:00-15:05 appear in both chunks
```

Dedup strategies:

| Strategy | Description |
|---|---|
| keep first chunk | sederhana |
| keep higher confidence | lebih baik jika confidence tersedia |
| match text similarity | hapus duplicate phrase |
| align by timestamp | merge overlapping words |
| manual review | jika konflik besar |

Pseudo-code:

```java id="f3d8n2"
public List<TranscriptWord> deduplicateOverlap(List<TranscriptWord> words) {
    List<TranscriptWord> sorted = words.stream()
            .sorted(Comparator.comparingLong(TranscriptWord::startMs))
            .toList();

    List<TranscriptWord> result = new ArrayList<>();

    for (TranscriptWord word : sorted) {
        boolean duplicate = result.stream().anyMatch(existing ->
                Math.abs(existing.startMs() - word.startMs()) < 500
                        && normalize(existing.text()).equals(normalize(word.text()))
        );

        if (!duplicate) {
            result.add(word);
        }
    }

    return result;
}
```

---

## 15. Speaker Label Consistency Across Chunks

Diarization labels can reset per chunk:

```text id="2gktrw"
chunk 0:
SPEAKER_1 = Alice-like voice
SPEAKER_2 = Bob-like voice

chunk 1:
SPEAKER_1 = Bob-like voice
SPEAKER_2 = Alice-like voice
```

If provider processes chunks independently, speaker label consistency can break.

Mitigations:

```text id="lt98aa"
1. Prefer provider diarization on full audio when possible
2. Avoid chunking when diarization quality is critical
3. Use overlap + speaker embedding matching if available
4. Let human reviewer relabel speaker turns
5. Use channel identification when audio channels are reliable
```

For first implementation, be honest in result metadata:

```json id="vmw1qh"
{
  "speakerLabelConsistency": "PER_CHUNK_NOT_GUARANTEED"
}
```

---

## 16. Overlapping Speech

Overlapping speech means two or more people speak at the same time.

```text id="4gv77l"
SPEAKER_1: I think we should-
SPEAKER_2: -sorry, can I add something?
```

Challenges:

```text id="zwk43z"
- ASR may miss one speaker
- diarization may assign to wrong speaker
- subtitle overlap may be hard to display
- exact timestamps may overlap
```

Representation:

```json id="ulxf1t"
{
  "segments": [
    {
      "speakerLabel": "SPEAKER_1",
      "startMs": 10000,
      "endMs": 12300,
      "text": "I think we should"
    },
    {
      "speakerLabel": "SPEAKER_2",
      "startMs": 11200,
      "endMs": 14000,
      "text": "sorry, can I add something"
    }
  ],
  "overlapDetected": true
}
```

Subtitle strategy:

| Strategy | Use |
|---|---|
| serialize by start time | simplest |
| include speaker labels | helps clarity |
| avoid overlapping cues in SRT | broader compatibility |
| allow overlapping cues in VTT if player supports | advanced |
| review overlap segments | high-value audio |

WebVTT cue timings are start/end offsets, and cues are ordered by start time; the spec notes that different cues can overlap. ([W3C](https://www.w3.org/2013/07/webvtt.html?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

## 17. Subtitle SRT Format

SRT is plain text with:

```text id="kzarl8"
1. numeric cue index
2. timing line
3. subtitle text
4. blank line
```

Library of Congress describes SRT as a text-based subtitle format containing subtitle text and timings; SRT files do not contain the audio or video themselves. ([The Library of Congress](https://www.loc.gov/preservation/digital/formats/fdd/fdd000569.shtml?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

Example:

```text id="p39hvh"
1
00:00:01,200 --> 00:00:04,600
Selamat pagi, kita mulai meeting.

2
00:00:05,000 --> 00:00:08,700
Baik, saya mulai dari agenda pertama.
```

SRT timestamp format:

```text id="zj0td8"
HH:MM:SS,mmm
```

---

## 18. Subtitle WebVTT Format

WebVTT starts with:

```text id="kuadkh"
WEBVTT
```

Cue example:

```text id="94k98s"
WEBVTT

00:00:01.200 --> 00:00:04.600
Selamat pagi, kita mulai meeting.

00:00:05.000 --> 00:00:08.700
Baik, saya mulai dari agenda pertama.
```

WebVTT is defined as text segments associated with time intervals called cues, and can be used for subtitles, captions, metadata, and chapters. ([W3C](https://www.w3.org/TR/webvtt1/?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

VTT timestamp format commonly uses:

```text id="vdvrpq"
HH:MM:SS.mmm
```

Difference from SRT:

| Aspect | SRT | WebVTT |
|---|---|---|
| Header | none required | `WEBVTT` required |
| Millisecond separator | comma | dot |
| Cue index | common/required by convention | optional |
| Browser text track | less native | native HTML5 track |
| Metadata/chapters | limited | supported use cases |
| Styling/positioning | limited | richer |

---

## 19. Subtitle Cue Generation Rules

A transcript segment is not always a good subtitle cue. Subtitle should be readable.

Recommended cue constraints:

```text id="o54fqy"
- duration: 1-7 seconds
- max chars per cue: 80-120
- max lines: 2
- max chars per line: 32-42
- avoid splitting numbers/names
- split at punctuation if possible
- preserve speaker labels if multi-speaker
```

Example long segment:

```text id="575297"
SPEAKER_1 00:10.000 - 00:22.000
Kita akan mulai dengan review dokumen invoice dan setelah itu masuk ke pembahasan hasil OCR yang perlu direview manual.
```

Subtitle split:

```text id="c6tb3i"
00:00:10.000 --> 00:00:15.000
[SPEAKER_1] Kita akan mulai dengan review
dokumen invoice,

00:00:15.000 --> 00:00:22.000
lalu masuk ke pembahasan hasil OCR
yang perlu direview manual.
```

---

## 20. Timestamp Formatting Code

```java id="98c8jc"
public final class SubtitleTimeFormatter {

    public static String formatSrt(long millis) {
        long hours = millis / 3_600_000;
        long minutes = (millis % 3_600_000) / 60_000;
        long seconds = (millis % 60_000) / 1_000;
        long ms = millis % 1_000;

        return String.format("%02d:%02d:%02d,%03d", hours, minutes, seconds, ms);
    }

    public static String formatVtt(long millis) {
        long hours = millis / 3_600_000;
        long minutes = (millis % 3_600_000) / 60_000;
        long seconds = (millis % 60_000) / 1_000;
        long ms = millis % 1_000;

        return String.format("%02d:%02d:%02d.%03d", hours, minutes, seconds, ms);
    }
}
```

---

## 21. SRT Generator

```java id="j5fxp6"
public final class SrtGenerator {

    public String generate(List<SubtitleCue> cues) {
        StringBuilder builder = new StringBuilder();

        int index = 1;

        for (SubtitleCue cue : cues) {
            builder.append(index++).append("\n");
            builder.append(SubtitleTimeFormatter.formatSrt(cue.startMs()))
                    .append(" --> ")
                    .append(SubtitleTimeFormatter.formatSrt(cue.endMs()))
                    .append("\n");
            builder.append(cue.text()).append("\n\n");
        }

        return builder.toString();
    }
}
```

---

## 22. WebVTT Generator

```java id="yars6b"
public final class WebVttGenerator {

    public String generate(List<SubtitleCue> cues) {
        StringBuilder builder = new StringBuilder();

        builder.append("WEBVTT").append("\n\n");

        for (SubtitleCue cue : cues) {
            builder.append(SubtitleTimeFormatter.formatVtt(cue.startMs()))
                    .append(" --> ")
                    .append(SubtitleTimeFormatter.formatVtt(cue.endMs()))
                    .append("\n");
            builder.append(cue.text()).append("\n\n");
        }

        return builder.toString();
    }
}
```

---

## 23. Subtitle Cue Builder

```java id="q787v5"
public final class SubtitleCueBuilder {

    private static final int MAX_CHARS = 90;
    private static final long MAX_DURATION_MS = 7000;

    public List<SubtitleCue> fromSegments(
            List<TranscriptSegment> segments,
            boolean includeSpeakerLabels
    ) {
        List<SubtitleCue> cues = new ArrayList<>();

        for (TranscriptSegment segment : segments) {
            List<SubtitleCue> segmentCues = splitSegment(segment, includeSpeakerLabels);
            cues.addAll(segmentCues);
        }

        for (int i = 0; i < cues.size(); i++) {
            SubtitleCue cue = cues.get(i);
            cues.set(i, new SubtitleCue(
                    i + 1,
                    cue.startMs(),
                    cue.endMs(),
                    cue.text(),
                    cue.speakerLabel(),
                    cue.format()
            ));
        }

        return cues;
    }

    private List<SubtitleCue> splitSegment(
            TranscriptSegment segment,
            boolean includeSpeakerLabels
    ) {
        String prefix = includeSpeakerLabels && segment.speakerLabel() != null
                ? "[" + segment.speakerLabel() + "] "
                : "";

        String text = prefix + segment.text();

        if (text.length() <= MAX_CHARS
                && segment.endMs() - segment.startMs() <= MAX_DURATION_MS) {
            return List.of(new SubtitleCue(
                    0,
                    segment.startMs(),
                    segment.endMs(),
                    lineBreak(text),
                    segment.speakerLabel(),
                    SubtitleFormat.SRT
            ));
        }

        return splitLongSegment(segment, prefix);
    }

    private String lineBreak(String text) {
        if (text.length() <= 42) {
            return text;
        }

        int midpoint = text.length() / 2;
        int split = text.lastIndexOf(' ', midpoint);

        if (split < 0) {
            return text;
        }

        return text.substring(0, split) + "\n" + text.substring(split + 1);
    }
}
```

For high quality, split by words/timestamps rather than only text length.

---

## 24. Word-Based Cue Splitting

Better approach:

```text id="dj36wn"
segment words:
word1 [start/end]
word2 [start/end]
...

Build cues:
- accumulate words until max chars/duration
- prefer punctuation boundary
- cue start = first word start
- cue end = last word end
```

Pseudo-code:

```java id="i1ss2b"
public List<SubtitleCue> buildFromWords(List<TranscriptWord> words) {
    List<SubtitleCue> cues = new ArrayList<>();
    List<TranscriptWord> current = new ArrayList<>();

    for (TranscriptWord word : words) {
        current.add(word);

        boolean tooLongText = joinWords(current).length() >= 90;
        boolean tooLongDuration = duration(current) >= 7000;
        boolean punctuationBoundary = word.text().matches(".*[.!?]$");

        if ((tooLongText || tooLongDuration) && punctuationBoundary) {
            cues.add(toCue(cues.size() + 1, current));
            current = new ArrayList<>();
        }
    }

    if (!current.isEmpty()) {
        cues.add(toCue(cues.size() + 1, current));
    }

    return cues;
}
```

---

## 25. Subtitle Artifact Storage

Generated subtitle files are artifacts.

```sql id="1d2ncs"
CREATE TABLE transcript_subtitle_artifact (
    id                         UUID PRIMARY KEY,
    transcription_result_id     UUID NOT NULL REFERENCES transcription_result(id),

    format                     VARCHAR(20) NOT NULL,
    language_code              VARCHAR(50),
    speaker_labels_included     BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT FALSE,

    asset_id                   UUID NOT NULL REFERENCES media_asset(id),
    cue_count                  INTEGER,
    generator_version          VARCHAR(100),

    created_at                 TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),

    UNIQUE (transcription_result_id, format, language_code, speaker_labels_included)
);
```

Artifact examples:

```text id="4wk3z4"
transcript-result.srt
transcript-result.vtt
transcript-speakers.srt
transcript-speakers.vtt
```

Retention should follow transcript sensitivity. Subtitle files can expose the same PII as transcript.

## 27. Speaker Review Workflow

Diarization often needs correction.

Reviewer actions:

```text id="mr96zu"
- rename speaker label
- assign role
- merge speakers
- split speaker label
- correct segment speaker
- mark overlapping speech
- mark inaudible
```

Example:

```text id="noc2w6"
Before:
SPEAKER_1 = sometimes Agent, sometimes Customer

After review:
SPEAKER_1 segment 1-20 → Agent
SPEAKER_1 segment 21-30 → Customer
```

Review table:

```sql id="qg7szx"
CREATE TABLE transcript_speaker_correction (
    id                         UUID PRIMARY KEY,
    transcription_result_id     UUID NOT NULL REFERENCES transcription_result(id),

    correction_type             VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL,
    old_speaker_label           VARCHAR(100),
    new_speaker_label           VARCHAR(100),

    segment_id                  UUID REFERENCES transcript_segment(id),
    start_ms                    BIGINT,
    end_ms                      BIGINT,

    reviewer_id                 UUID NOT NULL,
    reason                      TEXT,

    created_at                  TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now()
);
```

Correction types:

```text id="907vqp"
RENAME_SPEAKER
ASSIGN_ROLE
MERGE_SPEAKERS
SPLIT_SPEAKER
CHANGE_SEGMENT_SPEAKER
MARK_OVERLAP
MARK_INAUDIBLE
```

---

## 28. Transcript Text Correction

Text correction model:

```sql id="kz83lk"
CREATE TABLE transcript_text_correction (
    id                         UUID PRIMARY KEY,
    transcription_result_id     UUID NOT NULL REFERENCES transcription_result(id),
    segment_id                  UUID REFERENCES transcript_segment(id),
    word_id                     UUID REFERENCES transcript_word(id),

    correction_scope            VARCHAR(50) NOT NULL,

    new_text                    TEXT,

    start_ms                    BIGINT,
    end_ms                      BIGINT,

    reviewer_id                 UUID NOT NULL,
    reason                      TEXT,

    created_at                  TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now()
);
```

Scope:

```text id="9sk8do"
WORD
SEGMENT
TIME_RANGE
FULL_TRANSCRIPT
```

## 29. Timestamp Correction

Sometimes text is correct but timing is wrong.

```sql id="lu6a5p"
CREATE TABLE transcript_timing_correction (
    id                         UUID PRIMARY KEY,
    transcription_result_id     UUID NOT NULL REFERENCES transcription_result(id),
    segment_id                  UUID REFERENCES transcript_segment(id),
    word_id                     UUID REFERENCES transcript_word(id),

    old_start_ms                BIGINT,
    old_end_ms                  BIGINT,
    new_start_ms                BIGINT,
    new_end_ms                  BIGINT,

    reviewer_id                 UUID NOT NULL,
    reason                      TEXT,

    created_at                  TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now()
);
```

Validation:

```text id="rcl011"
- start < end
- no negative timestamps
- no excessive overlap unless allowed
- segment within media duration
- word inside segment
```

Transcript review UI should have:

```text id="95ep9q"
Audio/video player
  ├── waveform

  ├── search
  ├── low-confidence highlights
  ├── click segment → seek media
```

```text id="61a5h4"
Untuk pembayaran, kita perlu menunggu approval.
```

## 31. Search + Playback

Search result should include time range.

```json id="tflo7t"
{
  "query": "approval",
  "matches": [
    {
      "segmentId": "seg_42",
      "speakerLabel": "SPEAKER_2",
      "startMs": 84200,
      "endMs": 90100,
      "text": "Untuk pembayaran, kita perlu menunggu approval.",
      "highlight": "approval"
    }
  ]
}
```

Frontend:

```text id="op6p13"
click search result
→ player.seek(startMs - preRoll)
→ highlight segment
```

Pre-roll:

```text id="4bavtg"
start playback 1-2 seconds before segment
```

## 32. Speaker Analytics

Once diarization is reliable enough:

```text id="k12gqk"
- talk time per speaker
- number of turns
- average turn length
- interruption/overlap count
- silence duration
- agent/customer ratio
```

Example query:

```sql id="2ks51n"
SELECT
    speaker_label,
    SUM(end_ms - start_ms) AS talk_time_ms,
    COUNT(*) AS segment_count
FROM transcript_segment
WHERE transcription_result_id = :resultId
GROUP BY speaker_label
ORDER BY talk_time_ms DESC;
```

Caution:

```text id="dr6le7"
Do not use speaker analytics for performance evaluation unless diarization quality, consent, policy, and fairness have been reviewed.
```

---

## 33. Confidence Aggregation

Segment confidence:

```java id="8n9qnp"
public double segmentConfidence(List<TranscriptWord> words) {
    double totalDuration = 0;
    double weighted = 0;

    for (TranscriptWord word : words) {
        if (word.confidence() == null || word.startMs() == null || word.endMs() == null) {
            continue;
        }

        long duration = Math.max(1, word.endMs() - word.startMs());
        totalDuration += duration;
        weighted += word.confidence() * duration;
    }

    return totalDuration == 0 ? -1.0 : weighted / totalDuration;
}
```

Review triggers:

```text id="p03f8u"
- segment confidence below threshold
- unknown speaker label
- timestamp missing
- overlapping speech
- word confidence low around critical terms
- diarization provider warning
```
