## Response:
7/13/2026, 10:56:20 PM

# Seri 18/20 - Biometric Privacy, Consent, Audit, Retention, and Compliance Guardrails  
## Purpose Limitation, Lawful Basis, Deletion, Model/Provider Audit, Data Minimization, Human Review, dan Operational Controls

## 1. Introduction

Seri ini membahas **governance layer** untuk semua capability biometric/identity-related yang sudah kita bahas:

```text id="9rc0lq"
- face detection
- face verification
- face recognition / identification
- liveness detection
- face enrollment
- speaker identification
- voice biometric
- ID document + selfie matching
- biometric template / embedding storage
```

Di Indonesia, **data biometrik termasuk data pribadi yang bersifat spesifik** dalam UU No. 27 Tahun 2022 tentang Pelindungan Data Pribadi; naskah BPK mencantumkan data biometrik bersama data kesehatan, genetika, catatan kejahatan, data anak, dan data keuangan pribadi sebagai data pribadi spesifik. Prinsip UU PDP juga menekankan pengumpulan yang terbatas, spesifik, sah secara hukum, dan transparan, serta pemrosesan sesuai tujuan. ([Database Peraturan | JDIH BPK](https://peraturan.bpk.go.id/Details/229798/uu-no-27-tahun-2022?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

Catatan: ini bukan nasihat hukum. Untuk implementasi nyata, selalu sinkronkan desain teknis dengan legal, compliance, security, DPO/privacy officer, dan kebijakan internal perusahaan.

---

## 2. Mental Model

```text id="2qdt2z"
Biometric AI System
=
sensitive data processing
+ probabilistic model decision
+ identity/security impact
+ strict consent/purpose/retention/audit controls
```

Prinsip desain:

```text id="qsdvdq"
Jangan mulai dari:
"Bagaimana cara compare face?"

Mulai dari:
"Apakah kita boleh memproses biometric data untuk tujuan ini,
berapa lama harus disimpan,
siapa boleh akses,
apa alternatif non-biometric,
dan bagaimana user bisa menarik consent / minta deletion?"
```

---

## 3. Diagram Governance Layer

```text id="gxslop"
Biometric Capability
│
├── 1. Purpose & Legal Basis
│   ├── use case
│   ├── lawful basis / consent
│   ├── data subject notice
│   ├── policy approval
│   └── risk assessment / DPIA
│
├── 2. Data Minimization
│   ├── raw image needed?
│   ├── cropped face needed?
│   ├── embedding needed?
│   ├── provider face ID enough?
│   └── retention period
│
├── 3. Consent & Preference
│   ├── consent capture
│   ├── scope
│   ├── expiry
│   ├── withdrawal
│   ├── re-consent
│   └── deletion request
│
├── 4. Processing Controls
│   ├── quality gate
│   ├── liveness
│   ├── threshold policy
│   ├── human review
│   ├── access control
│   └── provider/model versioning
│
├── 5. Storage Controls
│   ├── encryption
│   ├── private object storage
│   ├── embedding protection
│   ├── key management
│   ├── retention_until
│   └── deletion/destruction evidence
│
├── 6. Audit & Monitoring
│   ├── who processed what
│   ├── purpose
│   ├── decision
│   ├── model/provider
│   ├── threshold
│   ├── reviewer action
│   └── access logs
│
└── 7. Operational Governance
    ├── incident response
    ├── provider review
    ├── model drift
    ├── fairness evaluation
    ├── false match review
    └── policy lifecycle
```

---

## 4. Practical Skill Map

| Area | Yang perlu dikuasai | Kenapa penting |
|---|---|---|
| Data classification | biometric, image, embedding, template, voiceprint | menentukan kontrol keamanan |
| Purpose limitation | tujuan pemrosesan eksplisit | mencegah function creep |
| Lawful basis / consent | dasar pemrosesan | mengurangi legal/compliance risk |
| Data minimization | simpan hanya yang perlu | mengurangi dampak breach |
| Retention | `retention_until`, deletion worker | mencegah data hidup selamanya |
| Consent withdrawal | revoke + stop processing | hak subjek data |
| Deletion/destruction | hapus raw, crop, embedding, provider ID | compliance dan trust |
| Threshold governance | threshold per use case | mencegah keputusan asal score |
| Human review | gray zone/high-impact | probabilistic result tidak auto-final |
| Audit trail | decision, access, correction | defensibility |
| Provider governance | region, terms, deletion API, model version | vendor risk |
| Security controls | encryption, KMS, private storage | biometric breach risk tinggi |
| Fairness/error evaluation | FMR/FNMR per segment jika relevan | risk control |
| Incident response | biometric leak tidak bisa “reset password” | severity tinggi |

---

## 5. Data Classification

Tidak semua data di face/biometric pipeline punya sensitivity yang sama, tetapi semuanya perlu dikontrol.

| Data Type | Example | Sensitivity | Default Control |
|---|---|---:|---|
| Raw selfie | uploaded face image | very high | private, encrypted, short retention |
| ID document photo | KTP/passport photo | very high | private, encrypted, strict access |
| Cropped face | face region crop | very high | private, short retention |
| Face embedding/template | vector biometric | very high | encrypt, minimize, avoid if possible |
| Provider face ID | external provider reference | high | treat as biometric pointer |
| Liveness video/session | camera capture | very high | short retention |
| Matching score | similarity score | high | audit-controlled |
| Detection bbox | coordinates | medium-high | controlled |
| Decision result | match/non-match | high | audit and access control |
| Human review correction | override decision | high | append-only audit |
| Voiceprint/speaker embedding | speaker identity vector | very high | biometric governance |

Practical rule:

```text id="b3h9vc"
Jika data dapat digunakan untuk mengidentifikasi atau memverifikasi seseorang,
perlakukan sebagai biometric-sensitive.
```

---

## 6. Purpose Limitation

Purpose limitation berarti data diproses hanya untuk tujuan yang dijelaskan dan disetujui.

Bad:

```text id="1j1fza"
Kita kumpulkan selfie untuk KYC,
lalu nanti dipakai juga untuk attendance, surveillance, fraud scoring,
dan training model tanpa consent/policy baru.
```

Good:

```text id="6otium"
Purpose:
"Selfie digunakan untuk memverifikasi kesesuaian wajah dengan foto identitas
dalam proses onboarding akun."

Not allowed without new approval:
- employee attendance
- marketing analytics
- public-space recognition
- model training
- 1:N search
```

Microsoft Azure Face privacy documentation menekankan bahwa biometric identification technology dapat memproses personal data yang mungkin dianggap sensitive personal information, dan hukum biometrik dapat berbeda antar yurisdiksi. Ini memperkuat kebutuhan purpose limitation dan legal review per use case. ([Microsoft Learn](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/foundry/responsible-ai/face/data-privacy-security?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

## 7. Consent Model

Consent harus granular.

```text id="4j5p2m"
Consent should answer:
- siapa subjek datanya?
- data apa yang dikumpulkan?
- untuk tujuan apa?
- siapa controller/processor?
- provider mana yang dipakai?
- disimpan berapa lama?
- bisa ditarik kapan?
- apa akibat jika tidak memberi consent?
```

### `biometric_consent`

```sql id="78uua3"
CREATE TABLE biometric_consent (
    id                      UUID PRIMARY KEY,
    tenant_id               UUID NOT NULL,
    subject_id              UUID NOT NULL,

    consent_type             VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL,
    purpose_key              VARCHAR(150) NOT NULL,

    status                  VARCHAR(50) NOT NULL,
    version                 INTEGER NOT NULL,

    notice_text_hash         TEXT NOT NULL,
    locale                  VARCHAR(20),

    granted_at              TIMESTAMPTZ,
    withdrawn_at            TIMESTAMPTZ,
    expires_at              TIMESTAMPTZ,

    granted_by              UUID,
    withdrawal_reason        TEXT,

    evidence_asset_id        UUID REFERENCES media_asset(id),
    metadata_json            JSONB,

    created_at              TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now()
);
```

Status:

```text id="qdyv7m"
GRANTED
WITHDRAWN
EXPIRED
REVOKED_BY_POLICY
SUPERSEDED
```

---

## 8. Consent Scope

Consent scope jangan terlalu luas.

| Scope | Example |
|---|---|
| `FACE_VERIFICATION_ONBOARDING` | selfie vs ID verification |
| `FACE_LIVENESS_CHECK` | anti-spoof check |
| `FACE_ENROLLMENT_ACCESS_CONTROL` | store template for access |
| `FACE_RECOGNITION_GALLERY_SEARCH` | 1:N search, restricted |
| `VOICE_BIOMETRIC_VERIFICATION` | verify speaker |
| `BIOMETRIC_HUMAN_REVIEW` | reviewer sees face/voice sample |
| `MODEL_IMPROVEMENT_TRAINING` | separate, usually opt-in |

Do not bundle:

```text id="xgbs83"
"I agree to all biometric processing"
```

Better:

```text id="hlm42i"
Separate consent / legal basis per purpose.
```

---

## 9. Lawful Basis and Jurisdiction Awareness

Untuk Indonesia, UU PDP menjadi baseline lokal; untuk EU/UK, biometric data used for unique identification is generally treated as special category data under GDPR/UK GDPR and requires a valid Article 9 condition in addition to ordinary lawful basis. The official EU GDPR text covers processing rules for personal data and special categories including biometric data for uniquely identifying a natural person. ([EUR-Lex](https://eur-lex.europa.eu/eli/reg/2016/679/oj/eng?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

Design implication:

```text id="7airm6"
Jangan hard-code "consent only" atau "legitimate interest only"
di code tanpa legal policy layer.

Buat legal_basis / purpose policy yang bisa dikonfigurasi per tenant, jurisdiction, dan use case.
```

---

## 10. Biometric Purpose Policy

```sql id="6dl3fp"
CREATE TABLE biometric_purpose_policy (
    id                          UUID PRIMARY KEY,
    policy_key                   VARCHAR(150) NOT NULL,

    jurisdiction                 VARCHAR(50),
    use_case                     VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL,
    biometric_type               VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL,

    allowed                      BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT FALSE,
    legal_basis                  VARCHAR(100),
    consent_required             BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT TRUE,
    human_review_required         BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT FALSE,

    allow_raw_image_storage       BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT FALSE,
    allow_embedding_storage       BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT FALSE,
    allow_provider_template        BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT FALSE,
    allow_one_to_many_search       BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT FALSE,
    allow_model_training           BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT FALSE,

    retention_days_raw_image       INTEGER,
    retention_days_crop            INTEGER,
    retention_days_embedding       INTEGER,
    retention_days_result          INTEGER,

    require_liveness               BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT FALSE,
    require_dpia                   BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT FALSE,
    require_security_approval       BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT TRUE,

    active                        BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT TRUE,
    created_at                    TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
    updated_at                    TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),

    UNIQUE (policy_key)
);
```

---

## 11. Data Minimization

Data minimization harus dijawab sebelum implementasi.

| Question | Good Default |
|---|---|
| Need raw selfie after verification? | No, delete after retention window |
| Need cropped face? | Only if review needed |
| Need embedding stored? | Avoid unless enrollment/search required |
| Need provider face ID? | Only if future matching required |
| Need liveness video? | Usually no long-term retention |
| Need full ID document? | Depends KYC/legal retention |
| Need result score? | Yes, for audit, but access-controlled |
| Need raw provider response? | Short retention/private artifact |

Example minimization:

```text id="eif6ad"
KYC selfie verification:
- store verification decision and audit
- store raw selfie for 7-30 days only if dispute/review needed
- do not store face embedding unless re-verification/enrollment required
- delete provider temporary face/template after result
```

---

## 12. Retention Policy

Biometric data harus punya `retention_until`.

### `biometric_artifact_retention`

```sql id="yg1tz1"
CREATE TABLE biometric_artifact_retention (
    id                      UUID PRIMARY KEY,
    asset_id                UUID NOT NULL REFERENCES media_asset(id),

    biometric_type           VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL,
    purpose_key              VARCHAR(150) NOT NULL,

    retention_policy_key     VARCHAR(150) NOT NULL,
    retention_until          TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL,

    legal_hold               BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT FALSE,
    deletion_status          VARCHAR(50) NOT NULL DEFAULT 'PENDING',

    deleted_at               TIMESTAMPTZ,
    deletion_evidence_json    JSONB,

    created_at               TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now()
);
```

Retention examples:

| Artifact | Suggested Policy Example |
|---|---|
| raw selfie for failed capture | delete quickly |
| raw selfie for completed KYC | short retention unless legal reason |
| cropped face for review | delete after review window |
| embedding for login/access enrollment | retain while consent/account active |
| liveness video | short retention |
| matching score/result | retain as audit per business/legal policy |
| deletion evidence | retain longer than artifact itself |

---

## 13. Deletion / Destruction Flow

Deletion harus menghapus semua turunan.

```text id="r6tp4w"
Delete biometric subject/template
  │
  ├── raw image asset
  ├── cropped face asset
  ├── liveness session/video
  ├── embedding encrypted blob
  ├── provider face/template ID
  ├── cache entry
  ├── search index/vector index
  ├── derived thumbnails/previews
  └── object storage versions if applicable
```

### Deletion event

```json id="gvhch4"
{
  "eventType": "BIOMETRIC_TEMPLATE_DELETED",
  "subjectId": "subj_123",
  "metadata": {
    "purpose": "FACE_ENROLLMENT_ACCESS_CONTROL",
    "deletedAssets": 4,
    "providerTemplatesDeleted": 1,
    "deletionEvidenceId": "del_456"
  }
}
```

Provider docs matter here. Azure Face data/privacy documentation specifically notes customers remain responsible for notice, retention/destruction notice, consent, and deletion obligations; provider-side processing does not remove the customer’s responsibility to manage lifecycle and legal duties. ([Microsoft Learn](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/foundry/responsible-ai/face/data-privacy-security?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

## 14. Storage Protection

Minimum controls:

```text id="mhbbyv"
- private bucket/container
- no public ACL
- encryption at rest
- KMS/customer-managed key where required
- short-lived signed URL
- strict tenant/case authorization
- object metadata classification
- access audit
- retention lifecycle
```

For embedding/template:

```text id="q7akuy"
- encrypt separately from normal metadata
- consider envelope encryption
- never log vector
- avoid returning embedding through API
- restrict DB read path
- use separate table/schema if needed
```

### `biometric_secret_ref`

```sql id="y36bh9"
CREATE TABLE biometric_secret_ref (
    id                      UUID PRIMARY KEY,
    tenant_id               UUID NOT NULL,

    secret_type              VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL,
    storage_provider         VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL,
    encrypted_ref            TEXT NOT NULL,

    kms_key_id               TEXT,
    algorithm                VARCHAR(100),

    created_at               TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
    rotated_at               TIMESTAMPTZ,
    deleted_at               TIMESTAMPTZ
);
```

---

## 15. Access Control

Access control harus berbasis purpose dan role.

| Role | View raw | View crop | View score | Override decision | Delete |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| normal app user | no | no | limited own result | no | request |
| case reviewer | limited | yes | yes | yes | no |
| senior reviewer | yes if needed | yes | yes | yes | no |
| security officer | yes for incident | yes | yes | no/yes by policy | no |
| privacy/DPO | audit view | maybe | yes | no | approve |
| system admin | no by default | no by default | no | no | ops only |
| deletion worker | no content read | no content read | no | no | execute deletion |

Do not give raw biometric read access to generic admin role.

---

## 16. API Guardrails

Every biometric API should enforce:

```text id="kp5lvn"
- authenticated caller
- tenant/case authorization
- purpose policy
- consent/legal basis
- allowed job type
- retention policy
- rate limit
- audit event
```

Example middleware flow:

```text id="y17jwv"
POST /face/verification-jobs
  │
  ├── authn/authz
  ├── load purpose policy
  ├── check consent
  ├── validate source assets
  ├── check biometric processing allowed
  ├── create job
  └── audit BIOMETRIC_PROCESSING_REQUESTED
```

---

## 17. Consent Check Service

```java id="y69ikm"
public final class BiometricConsentService {

    private final BiometricConsentRepository consentRepository;
    private final BiometricPurposePolicyRepository policyRepository;

    public ConsentDecision check(
            UUID tenantId,
            UUID subjectId,
            String purposeKey,
            String biometricType
    ) {
        BiometricPurposePolicy policy =
                policyRepository.getActive(purposeKey, biometricType);

        if (!policy.allowed()) {
            return ConsentDecision.denied("PURPOSE_NOT_ALLOWED");
        }

        if (!policy.consentRequired()) {
            return ConsentDecision.allowed("CONSENT_NOT_REQUIRED_BY_POLICY");
        }

        Optional<BiometricConsent> consent =
                consentRepository.findActive(tenantId, subjectId, purposeKey);

        if (consent.isEmpty()) {
            return ConsentDecision.denied("CONSENT_MISSING");
        }

        if (consent.get().isExpired()) {
            return ConsentDecision.denied("CONSENT_EXPIRED");
        }

        return ConsentDecision.allowed("CONSENT_GRANTED");
    }
}
```

---

## 18. Human Review Guardrails

Human review bukan free pass. Reviewer harus punya batasan.

```text id="xc84ng"
Reviewer can:
- confirm match/non-match in gray zone
- request recapture
- reject poor quality
- escalate
- correct metadata

Reviewer should not:
- download raw biometric arbitrarily
- override policy disallowing processing
- use data for new purpose
- access unrelated tenant/case
```

Review decision harus append-only:

```sql id="p0tqmo"
CREATE TABLE biometric_review_decision (
    id                      UUID PRIMARY KEY,
    job_id                  UUID NOT NULL,

    reviewer_id             UUID NOT NULL,
    decision                VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL,
    reason_code             VARCHAR(100),
    comment                 TEXT,

    previous_decision        VARCHAR(100),
    new_decision             VARCHAR(100),

    reviewed_at             TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
    metadata_json            JSONB
);
```

---

## 19. Automated Decision Guardrail

Face/biometric matching is probabilistic. AWS Rekognition’s responsible AI service card and API docs emphasize that face matching returns similarity scores and that human review is recommended before decisions that affect rights, privacy, or access. ([AWS Documentation](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/ai/responsible-ai/rekognition-face-matching/overview.html?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

Design rule:

```text id="hxk838"
For high-impact decisions:
- no score-only final decision
- use calibrated thresholds
- use liveness where relevant
- use gray-zone review
- provide appeal/retry/recapture path
- audit decision path
```

Decision matrix:

| Scenario | Auto? | Guardrail |
|---|---:|---|
| blur all faces in image | yes | privacy-positive, low risk |
| selfie vs ID for onboarding | limited | liveness + threshold + review |
| unlock normal app login | prefer WebAuthn | avoid server biometric |
| 1:N employee access | very restricted | policy + consent + review/monitoring |
| public-space identification | avoid/default no | high-risk |
| law enforcement-like search | legal/compliance escalation | strict controls |

---

## 20. Model / Provider Audit

Persist every decision’s technical context.

```sql id="zbmd75"
CREATE TABLE biometric_processing_audit (
    id                      UUID PRIMARY KEY,
    tenant_id               UUID NOT NULL,
    subject_id              UUID,

    job_id                  UUID NOT NULL,
    job_type                VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL,
    purpose_key             VARCHAR(150) NOT NULL,

    provider_name            VARCHAR(100),
    model_name               TEXT,
    model_version            TEXT,
    algorithm_version        TEXT,

    threshold_policy_key     VARCHAR(150),
    accept_threshold          NUMERIC(8,5),
    reject_threshold          NUMERIC(8,5),

    similarity_score          NUMERIC(8,5),
    liveness_decision         VARCHAR(100),
    final_decision            VARCHAR(100),

    consent_id               UUID,
    human_review_required     BOOLEAN,
    human_review_id           UUID,

    source_asset_ids          UUID[],
    derived_asset_ids         UUID[],

    created_at               TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
    metadata_json             JSONB
);
```

Why:

```text id="a19iq6"
6 bulan kemudian, Anda harus bisa menjawab:
- model/provider apa yang dipakai?
- threshold berapa?
- score berapa?
- consent mana?
- reviewer siapa?
- raw/cropped asset mana?
- data sudah dihapus atau belum?
```

---

## 21. Provider Governance Checklist

Before using AWS/Azure/Google/on-prem provider, document:

```text id="8lsumd"
- provider name
- service capability used
- region
- data residency
- whether data is retained by provider
- deletion API/process
- model version visibility
- logging behavior
- subprocessors
- support for customer-managed keys
- SLA/availability
- responsible AI documentation
- restrictions on use cases
```

Azure Face docs explicitly warn that biometric identification laws vary and customers must consider applicable obligations; AWS provides AI Service Cards for Rekognition face matching to document responsible-use considerations. ([Microsoft Learn](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/foundry/responsible-ai/face/data-privacy-security?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

## 22. DPIA / Risk Assessment Trigger

A Data Protection Impact Assessment or similar risk review should be triggered for high-risk biometric processing.

Trigger examples:

```text id="itatzg"
- large-scale biometric processing
- 1:N recognition/search
- access control using biometric
- children/minors
- public or semi-public capture
- automated high-impact decision
- cross-border transfer
- model training using biometric data
- new provider/model
- expanded purpose
```

Under Indonesia’s PDP context, biometric data is specific personal data, and secondary summaries of the PDP Law note additional safeguards for specific personal data such as DPIA/DPO-type governance in large-scale processing contexts; treat this as a compliance item to verify with legal rather than leaving it as engineering discretion. ([Database Peraturan | JDIH BPK](https://peraturan.bpk.go.id/Details/229798/uu-no-27-tahun-2022?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

## 23. Biometric Processing Registry

Maintain registry of all biometric use cases.

```sql id="c6fv3m"
CREATE TABLE biometric_processing_registry (
    id                          UUID PRIMARY KEY,

    use_case_key                 VARCHAR(150) NOT NULL,
    description                  TEXT NOT NULL,
    owner_team                   VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL,

    biometric_type               VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL,
    purpose_key                  VARCHAR(150) NOT NULL,

    data_controller              TEXT,
    data_processor               TEXT,
    provider_name                VARCHAR(100),

    jurisdiction                 VARCHAR(50),
    legal_basis                  VARCHAR(100),
    consent_required             BOOLEAN NOT NULL,

    dpia_required                BOOLEAN NOT NULL,
    dpia_status                  VARCHAR(50),

    security_review_status        VARCHAR(50),
    privacy_review_status         VARCHAR(50),

    active                      BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT FALSE,

    created_at                  TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
    updated_at                  TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),

    UNIQUE (use_case_key)
);
```

---

## 24. Retention Worker

```java id="d4gkbk"
public final class BiometricRetentionWorker {

    private final BiometricRetentionRepository retentionRepository;
    private final ObjectStorageClient storageClient;
    private final ProviderTemplateDeletionService providerDeletionService;
    private final AuditService auditService;

    public void runOnce() {
        List<BiometricArtifactRetention> dueItems =
                retentionRepository.findDueForDeletion(Instant.now(), 100);

        for (BiometricArtifactRetention item : dueItems) {
            if (item.legalHold()) {
                continue;
            }

            try {
                storageClient.deleteObject(item.assetId());

                providerDeletionService.deleteIfProviderTemplate(item.assetId());

                retentionRepository.markDeleted(
                        item.id(),
                        DeletionEvidence.success()
                );

                auditService.record("BIOMETRIC_ARTIFACT_DELETED", item.assetId());

            } catch (Exception e) {
                retentionRepository.markDeletionFailed(item.id(), e.getMessage());
                auditService.record("BIOMETRIC_ARTIFACT_DELETION_FAILED", item.assetId());
            }
        }
    }
}
```

---

## 25. Withdrawal Flow

```text id="qo2c52"
User withdraws consent
  │
  ▼
mark consent WITHDRAWN
  │
  ▼
stop future processing for that purpose
  │
  ▼
find active biometric templates/artifacts under that consent/purpose
  │
  ├── delete raw/crop/embedding/provider template if no legal hold
  ├── retain minimal audit result if allowed/required
  └── publish consent withdrawn event
```

API:

```http id="5wuxge"
POST /biometric-consents/{consentId}/withdraw
```

```json id="49jqrr"
{
  "reason": "USER_REQUEST"
}
```

Response:

```json id="8te6yd"
{
  "status": "WITHDRAWN",
  "deletionJobId": "del_job_123"
}
```

---

## 26. Data Subject Access / Export

When allowed by policy/legal process, provide understandable summary:

```json id="lp4roe"
{
  "subjectId": "user_123",
  "biometricProcessing": [
    {
      "purpose": "FACE_VERIFICATION_ONBOARDING",
      "status": "COMPLETED",
      "processedAt": "2026-07-13T10:00:00+07:00",
      "provider": "AZURE_FACE",
      "decision": "MATCH",
      "rawImageRetentionUntil": "2026-08-12T00:00:00+07:00",
      "embeddingStored": false
    }
  ]
}
```

Do not casually export embeddings/templates. Provide human-readable data and follow legal/security workflow.

---

## 27. Audit Event Taxonomy

```text id="9kw2ro"
BIOMETRIC_CONSENT_GRANTED
BIOMETRIC_CONSENT_WITHDRAWN
BIOMETRIC_PROCESSING_REQUESTED
BIOMETRIC_PURPOSE_POLICY_CHECKED
BIOMETRIC_PROCESSING_ALLOWED
BIOMETRIC_PROCESSING_DENIED
BIOMETRIC_ASSET_CREATED
BIOMETRIC_TEMPLATE_CREATED
BIOMETRIC_TEMPLATE_MATCHED
BIOMETRIC_LIVENESS_CHECKED

BIOMETRIC_HUMAN_REVIEW_REQUIRED
BIOMETRIC_HUMAN_REVIEW_COMPLETED

BIOMETRIC_ARTIFACT_DELETED
BIOMETRIC_PROVIDER_TEMPLATE_DELETED
BIOMETRIC_RETENTION_EXPIRED
BIOMETRIC_POLICY_UPDATED
```

Audit event example:

```json id="j00cbe"
{
  "eventType": "BIOMETRIC_DECISION_MADE",
  "tenantId": "tnt_001",
  "subjectId": "user_123",
  "jobId": "job_face_789",

    "provider": "AZURE_FACE",

    "decision": "MATCH",
    "thresholdPolicy": "KYC_SELFIE_ID_V1",

    "humanReviewRequired": false
  }
}
```

## 28. Access Audit

Every access to raw/cropped biometric media should be logged.

```sql id="ch1lp4"
CREATE TABLE biometric_access_log (
    id                      UUID PRIMARY KEY,
    tenant_id               UUID NOT NULL,
    actor_id                UUID NOT NULL,

    asset_id                UUID REFERENCES media_asset(id),
    subject_id              UUID,

    access_type             VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL,
    purpose_key             VARCHAR(150) NOT NULL,

    case_id                 VARCHAR(100),
    reason                  TEXT,

    ip_address              INET,
    user_agent              TEXT,

    allowed                 BOOLEAN NOT NULL,
    denial_reason           VARCHAR(100),

    created_at              TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now()
);
```

Access types:

```text id="1mdzi5"
VIEW_RAW_IMAGE
VIEW_CROPPED_FACE
DOWNLOAD_ARTIFACT
VIEW_MATCH_SCORE
VIEW_PROVIDER_RESPONSE
EXPORT_AUDIT
DELETE_ARTIFACT
```

---

## 29. Incident Response

Biometric breach is high severity because biometric identifiers cannot be rotated like passwords.

Incident playbook should include:

```text id="hbev9c"
- identify affected subjects
- identify data types leaked
- raw image vs crop vs embedding vs provider ID
- revoke provider templates if applicable
- disable affected galleries
- rotate keys/secrets
- stop processing if needed
- notify legal/compliance
- preserve audit logs
- execute notification obligations
- post-incident threshold/access review
```

NIST describes biometric security work as high-stakes because facial recognition is used in contexts such as device unlocking and identity at borders, and NIST evaluates accuracy and attacks on facial recognition technology. ([NIST](https://www.nist.gov/blogs/taking-measure/facing-facts-keep-our-biometrics-secure?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

## 30. Fairness / Error Monitoring

NIST’s Face Recognition Vendor Test and demographic evaluations are widely used to measure face recognition accuracy and demographic effects; NIST has reported that false match and false non-match behavior can vary by algorithm, image quality, and demographic grouping. ([NIST](https://www.nist.gov/programs-projects/face-recognition-vendor-test-frvt?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

Engineering actions:

```text id="3p78ed"
- track false reject reports
- track human override rate
- evaluate threshold by use case
- sample review across demographic groups where lawful/appropriate
- compare provider/model versions before rollout
- do not rely on one global accuracy number
```

Metrics:

| Metric | Meaning |
|---|---|
| false accept report count | alleged wrong match accepted |
| false reject report count | legitimate user rejected |
| recapture rate | capture quality issue |
| liveness fail rate | spoof/UX issue |
| human override rate | model/threshold quality |
| gray-zone rate | threshold calibration |
| provider disagreement rate | multi-provider quality signal |
| deletion SLA breach | governance failure |

---

## 31. Threshold Change Management

Threshold is a policy artifact, not a random config.

```sql id="40m2oy"
CREATE TABLE biometric_threshold_policy_version (
    id                      UUID PRIMARY KEY,
    policy_key              VARCHAR(150) NOT NULL,
    version                 INTEGER NOT NULL,

    provider_name            VARCHAR(100),
    model_name               TEXT,
    model_version            TEXT,

    accept_threshold          NUMERIC(8,5) NOT NULL,
    reject_threshold          NUMERIC(8,5) NOT NULL,

    justification             TEXT NOT NULL,
    evaluation_report_asset_id UUID REFERENCES media_asset(id),

    approved_by              UUID NOT NULL,
    approved_at              TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL,

    active_from              TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL,
    active_until             TIMESTAMPTZ,

    UNIQUE (policy_key, version)
);
```

Change process:

```text id="e93egd"
propose threshold
→ evaluate on dataset
→ estimate FMR/FNMR
→ privacy/security review
→ approve version
→ rollout gradually
→ monitor override/incident metrics
```

---

## 32. Provider / Model Version Rollout

Before changing model/provider:

```text id="wngevm"
- run shadow evaluation
- compare score distributions
- compare false reject reports
- compare human review rate
- verify deletion/retention behavior
- update threshold policy if score scale differs
- store new model/provider version
```

Never assume:

```text id="7dm6zq"
Provider A score 0.90 == Provider B score 0.90
```

Scores are not universally comparable.

---

## 33. Training / Model Improvement Guardrails

Using biometric data for model improvement is a separate purpose.

Default:

```text id="gqdxzf"
allow_model_training = false
```

If allowed:

```text id="s1l22m"
- separate consent/legal basis
- de-identification/pseudonymization where possible
- dataset registry
- retention period
- access approval
- evaluation report
- deletion propagation
- no export to uncontrolled environment
```

### `biometric_dataset_registry`

```sql id="lemytg"
CREATE TABLE biometric_dataset_registry (
    id                      UUID PRIMARY KEY,
    dataset_key              VARCHAR(150) NOT NULL,

    purpose_key              VARCHAR(150) NOT NULL,
    biometric_type           VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL,

    source_job_ids           UUID[],
    asset_count              INTEGER,
    subject_count            INTEGER,

    consent_scope_verified    BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT FALSE,
    deidentification_status   VARCHAR(100),

    storage_location          TEXT,
    retention_until           TIMESTAMPTZ,

    approved_by              UUID,
    approved_at              TIMESTAMPTZ,

    created_at               TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),

    UNIQUE (dataset_key)
);
```

---

## 34. Logging Rules

Never log:

```text id="6pvq7j"
- raw biometric image URL with token
- embedding vector
- provider face/template ID in public logs
- full ID document OCR text
- liveness video path
- raw provider response if it contains sensitive data
```

Allowed structured log:

```json id="1i7e9b"
{
  "event": "face_verification_completed",
  "tenantId": "tnt_001",
  "jobId": "job_face_789",
  "provider": "AZURE_FACE",
  "decision": "REVIEW_REQUIRED",
  "thresholdPolicy": "KYC_SELFIE_ID_V1",
  "durationMs": 820
}
```

---

## 35. Secure Review UI

Review UI must minimize exposure.

Requirements:

```text id="1op3va"
- short-lived signed image URL
- watermark "review only" if needed
- no browser caching if policy requires
- restrict download
- mask unrelated document areas
- audit every view
- disable right-click is not security, but can reduce accidental misuse
- session timeout
- reviewer reason code required
```

Reviewer should see:

```text id="ccfciv"
- cropped reference face
- cropped probe face
- quality flags
- score band, not necessarily full model internals
- liveness status
- threshold policy
- allowed decisions
```

---

## 36. Common Anti-Patterns

| Anti-pattern | Dampak | Alternatif |
|---|---|---|
| Store all selfies forever | privacy/storage risk | retention_until + deletion worker |
| Use one broad consent | weak governance | purpose-specific consent |
| Reuse KYC selfie for surveillance | function creep | new purpose approval |
| Store embeddings by default | breach impact | avoid unless required |
| Admin can browse biometric images | insider risk | purpose/role access |
| No deletion propagation to provider | incomplete erasure | provider deletion workflow |
| Threshold changed silently | audit/performance drift | versioned threshold policy |
| Recognition enabled by default | high-risk misuse | explicit policy only |
| Log provider raw response | sensitive leakage | sanitized audit |
| Auto-decide high-impact score-only | false decision risk | human review/appeal |
| No incident playbook | slow breach response | biometric incident plan |
| Model training without consent | legal/trust risk | separate consent/registry |

---

## 38. Minimal Implementation untuk Tahap Awal

Implementasi minimal yang aman:

```text id="f7zg4s"
1. biometric_purpose_policy table
2. biometric_consent table
3. consent check service
4. retention_until for raw/cropped biometric assets
5. biometric retention/deletion worker
6. threshold policy versioning
7. biometric_processing_audit table
8. biometric_access_log table
9. provider/model metadata persistence
10. gray-zone human review
11. no 1:N recognition by default
12. no embedding storage unless explicitly approved
```

Default safe policy:

```text id="kegh26"
- allow face verification 1:1 only for approved KYC/onboarding
- require liveness for remote verification
- store decision/audit
- short retention for raw/cropped images
- do not store embedding
- do not allow 1:N search
- require human review in gray zone
```

Urutan berikutnya:

```text id="crfsza"
Seri 19 → Provider Abstraction: AWS/Azure/Google/on-prem engine switching
Seri 20 → Production Hardening: observability, cost control, queue lag, GPU/Kubernetes, failure handling
```

Biometric governance bukan tambahan opsional. Untuk senior engineer, biometric system yang bagus bukan hanya “akurasi tinggi”, tetapi **purpose-limited, consent-aware, minimized, encrypted, reviewable, deletable, auditable, dan operable**.

## Response:
7/13/2026, 10:59:04 PM

# Seri 19/20 - Provider Abstraction  
## AWS / Azure / Google / On-Prem Engine Switching untuk OCR, STT, TTS, Face, Document AI, Media Processing, Fallback Strategy, Contract Model, Cost Routing, dan Vendor Lock-in Control

## 1. Introduction

**Provider abstraction** adalah desain agar aplikasi Anda tidak terkunci ke satu vendor atau satu engine untuk OCR, Document AI, Speech-to-Text, Text-to-Speech, Face AI, dan media processing.

Tanpa abstraction, business service akan penuh kode seperti:

```text id="c0n3qn"
if provider == AWS then parse Textract Blocks
if provider == Azure then parse AnalyzeResult
if provider == Google then parse Document object
if provider == Tesseract then parse TSV
```

Itu akan cepat menjadi vendor lock-in, sulit dites, sulit fallback, sulit audit, dan sulit dikontrol cost-nya.

Dengan abstraction:

```text id="ck94jt"
Business Workflow
  │
  ▼
Canonical Capability Interface
  │
  ▼
Provider Router / Policy
  │
  ├── AWS adapter
  ├── Azure adapter
  ├── Google adapter
  ├── On-prem adapter
  └── Local tool adapter
  │
  ▼
Canonical Result
```

Cloud provider memang punya capability kuat tetapi shape output-nya berbeda. Misalnya, Amazon Textract dapat mengekstrak table structures seperti cells, merged cells, column headers, titles, footers, dan table type; Azure Document Intelligence general document model mengekstrak key-value pairs, tables, selection marks, text, dan structure; Google Document AI Enterprise Document OCR mengekstrak text dan layout information dari dokumen. ([AWS Documentation](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/textract/latest/dg/how-it-works-tables.html?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

## 2. Mental Model

```text id="zmmr95"
Provider abstraction bukan sekadar interface Java.

Provider abstraction =
canonical request/result contract
+ provider adapter
+ routing policy
+ capability matrix
+ fallback strategy
+ cost/latency/data-residency rules
+ audit metadata
+ versioning
+ observability
```

Tujuannya bukan membuat semua provider terlihat identik secara palsu. Tujuannya adalah:

```text id="q5phmw"
- business workflow tidak tahu detail provider
- provider-specific detail tetap tersimpan untuk audit/debug
- result dinormalisasi ke canonical model
- fallback bisa dilakukan secara aman
- cost dan SLA bisa dikontrol
- migration provider tidak menghancurkan API
```

---

## 3. Big Picture Diagram

```text id="7zr08f"
Jersey API / Camunda Workflow
        │
        ▼
Capability Service
        │
        ├── OcrService
        ├── DocumentAiService
        ├── SpeechToTextService
        ├── TextToSpeechService
        ├── FaceAiService
        └── MediaProcessingService
        │
        ▼
Provider Router
        │
        ├── reads provider_policy
        ├── checks capability matrix
        ├── checks tenant / region / cost / SLA
        ├── checks fallback rules
        └── selects provider adapter
        │
        ▼
Provider Adapter
        │
        ├── AWS
        ├── Azure
        ├── Google
        ├── On-prem
        ├── Local binary
        └── Local model
        │
        ▼
Canonical Result Normalizer
        │
        ▼
PostgreSQL + Object Storage + Audit
```

---

## 4. Practical Skill Map

| Area | Yang perlu dikuasai | Kenapa penting |
|---|---|---|
| Capability boundary | OCR vs Document AI vs STT vs TTS vs Face | Jangan satu interface untuk semua |
| Canonical contract | request/result internal stabil | Workflow tidak vendor-specific |
| Provider adapter | wrapper AWS/Azure/Google/local | Isolasi SDK/API detail |
| Normalizer | provider result → canonical result | Result konsisten |
| Raw artifact storage | simpan provider raw response private | Audit/debug |
| Capability matrix | fitur apa didukung provider mana | Routing aman |
| Provider policy | tenant/region/cost/SLA/provider priority | Runtime decision |
| Fallback | provider gagal → alternatif | Reliability |
| Circuit breaker | provider bermasalah → stop sementara | Menghindari cascading failure |
| Cost routing | pilih provider berdasarkan cost/quality | Budget control |
| Data residency | region/on-prem constraints | Compliance |
| Versioning | provider/model/schema version | Audit dan migration |
| Golden tests | provider result fixture | Adapter regression |
| Observability | latency/error/cost/result quality | Operability |

---

## 5. Capability Families

Jangan buat satu interface `AiProvider` raksasa. Pisahkan berdasarkan capability.

```text id="8bj2be"
CapabilityProvider
├── OcrProvider
├── DocumentAiProvider
├── SpeechToTextProvider
├── StreamingSpeechProvider
├── TextToSpeechProvider
├── FaceAiProvider
├── LivenessProvider
├── MediaProcessingProvider
└── RedactionProvider
```

Kenapa dipisah?

```text id="ghp5uc"
- input/output berbeda
- timeout berbeda
- cost berbeda
- retry berbeda
- privacy risk berbeda
- provider support berbeda
- result quality metric berbeda
```

---

## 6. Provider Capability Matrix

| Capability | AWS | Azure | Google | On-prem / Local |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Basic OCR | Textract | Document Intelligence Read/Layout | Document AI OCR | Tesseract/Tess4J |
| Layout OCR | Textract Layout/Tables/Forms | Layout/General Document | Enterprise Document OCR | Tesseract TSV/hOCR + rules |
| Document AI | Textract AnalyzeDocument/Adapters | Document Intelligence custom/prebuilt | Document AI processors | Custom model/rules |
| Batch STT | Amazon Transcribe | Azure Batch Transcription | Speech-to-Text long-running | Whisper/local ASR |
| Streaming STT | Transcribe Streaming | Azure Speech SDK | Streaming Recognize | local streaming ASR |
| TTS | Amazon Polly | Azure Speech TTS | Cloud Text-to-Speech | local TTS |
| Face detection/verification | Rekognition | Azure Face | limited/varies by Google APIs | OpenCV/ONNX/custom |
| Media processing | MediaConvert optional / FFmpeg in AWS | Azure Media/FFmpeg in worker | Transcoder API/FFmpeg | FFmpeg |
| Object storage | S3 | Blob Storage | Cloud Storage | MinIO/NFS/object store |

AWS Transcribe distinguishes batch transcription from streaming transcription in SDK docs; batch jobs use media located in S3, while streaming uses real-time audio. AWS `StartTranscriptionJob` specifically requires media file location in S3. ([AWS Documentation](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/transcribe/latest/dg/getting-started-sdk.html?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

## 7. Provider Abstraction Layers

Recommended layers:

```text id="fxyzt4"
Business Service
  │
  ▼
Capability Interface
  │
  ▼
Provider Router
  │
  ▼
Provider Adapter
  │
  ▼
SDK / REST / CLI / Local model
  │
  ▼
Provider Raw Result
  │
  ▼
Normalizer
  │
  ▼
Canonical Result
```

Example:

```text id="5yxosm"
DocumentAiService.extractInvoice()
  │
  ▼
DocumentAiProvider.analyze()
  │
  ▼
AwsTextractDocumentAiProvider
  │
  ▼
Textract AnalyzeDocument
  │
  ▼
Textract Blocks
  │
  ▼
AwsTextractNormalizer
  │
  ▼
CanonicalDocumentAiResult
```

---

## 8. Canonical Request / Result Principle

### Bad design

```java id="mefn57"
public TextractResponse analyzeInvoice(TextractRequest request);
```

Masalah:

```text id="cv78sr"
- business code tahu Textract
- migration susah
- Azure/Google/on-prem tidak cocok
- test fixture vendor-specific
```

### Good design

```java id="6s8jv9"
public DocumentAiResult analyze(DocumentAiRequest request);
```

Provider raw result tetap boleh disimpan, tetapi tidak menjadi public contract.

---

## 9. Canonical Provider Metadata

Setiap canonical result harus punya provider metadata.

```java id="d5zatx"
public record ProviderMetadata(
        String providerName,
        String providerRegion,
        String providerRequestId,
        String modelName,
        String modelVersion,
        String adapterVersion,
        String normalizerVersion,
        Map<String, Object> rawSummary
) {}
```

Persist metadata untuk audit:

```json id="gso1bi"
{
  "providerName": "AWS_TEXTRACT",
  "providerRegion": "ap-southeast-1",
  "providerRequestId": "abc-123",
  "modelName": "AnalyzeDocument",
  "modelVersion": "provider-managed",
  "adapterVersion": "aws-textract-adapter-v3",
  "normalizerVersion": "document-ai-normalizer-v2"
}
```

---

## 10. OCR Provider Interface

```java id="ab8frp"
public interface OcrProvider {

    OcrResult extractText(OcrRequest request);

    ProviderCapabilityDescriptor capabilities();
}
```

```java id="lx1kg2"
public record OcrRequest(
        UUID jobId,
        UUID sourceAssetId,
        URI inputUri,
        String languageHint,
        boolean returnWords,
        boolean returnBoundingBoxes,
        boolean preserveRawResult,
        Map<String, Object> options
) {}
```

```java id="q3wg4d"
public record OcrResult(
        UUID jobId,
        String fullText,
        List<OcrPage> pages,
        double meanConfidence,
        ProviderMetadata providerMetadata,
        UUID rawProviderAssetId
) {}
```

Implementations:

```text id="ywcsrd"
OcrProvider
├── TesseractOcrProvider
├── AwsTextractOcrProvider
├── AzureReadOcrProvider
└── GoogleDocumentOcrProvider
```

## 11. Document AI Provider Interface

```java id="0ezhj0"
public interface DocumentAiProvider {

    DocumentAiResult analyze(DocumentAiRequest request);

    ProviderCapabilityDescriptor capabilities();
}
```

```java id="v98zd4"
public record DocumentAiRequest(
        UUID jobId,
        UUID sourceAssetId,
        URI documentUri,
        String expectedDocumentType,
        String schemaVersion,
        boolean extractLayout,
        boolean extractTables,
        boolean extractKeyValuePairs,
        boolean extractSelectionMarks,
        boolean preserveRawProviderResponse,
        Map<String, Object> options
) {}
```

```java id="aiytuv"
public record DocumentAiResult(
        UUID jobId,
        String documentType,
        String schemaVersion,
        CanonicalDocumentLayout layout,
        Map<String, ExtractedField> fields,
        Map<String, ExtractedTable> tables,
        DocumentValidationResult validation,
        ProviderMetadata providerMetadata,
        UUID rawProviderAssetId
) {}
```

Amazon Textract supports queries in `AnalyzeDocument` so callers can ask for specific document information, while Azure’s general document model can extract key-value pairs, tables, selection marks, text, and structure from structured, semi-structured, and unstructured documents. ([AWS Documentation](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/textract/latest/dg/queryresponse.html?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

## 12. Speech-to-Text Provider Interface

```java id="gwn3mc"
public interface SpeechToTextProvider {

    ProviderJob submitBatch(TranscriptionRequest request);

    ProviderJobStatus getStatus(String providerJobId);

    TranscriptionResult fetchResult(String providerJobId);

    ProviderCapabilityDescriptor capabilities();
}
```

Implementations:

```text id="tehhue"
SpeechToTextProvider
├── AwsTranscribeProvider
├── AzureBatchSpeechProvider
├── GoogleSpeechToTextProvider
└── LocalWhisperProvider
```

Azure Batch Transcription docs describe submitting audio data in batch, letting the service transcribe it asynchronously, storing results in a storage container, and retrieving results afterward. ([AWS Documentation](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/transcribe/latest/dg/getting-started-sdk.html?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

## 13. Text-to-Speech Provider Interface

```java id="p0bh00"
public interface TextToSpeechProvider {

    TtsResult synthesize(TtsRequest request);

    List<TtsVoiceInfo> listVoices(TtsVoiceQuery query);

    ProviderCapabilityDescriptor capabilities();
}
```

```java id="q1z7g5"
public record TtsRequest(
        UUID jobId,
        String inputType,
        String textOrSsml,
        String locale,
        String voiceProfileKey,
        String providerVoiceName,
        String outputFormat,
        Integer sampleRateHz,
        boolean generateSpeechMarks,
        Map<String, Object> options
) {}
```

Amazon Polly `SynthesizeSpeech` supports speech synthesis and speech marks have JSON output constraints; Azure TTS uses SSML in the REST request body and output format is selected via `X-Microsoft-OutputFormat`; Google Cloud TTS converts text or SSML input into audio data. ([AWS Documentation](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/polly/latest/APIReference/API_SynthesizeSpeech.html?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

## 14. Face AI Provider Interface

```java id="rv29ht"
public interface FaceAiProvider {

    FaceDetectionResult detectFaces(FaceDetectionRequest request);

    FaceVerificationResult verifyFace(FaceVerificationRequest request);

    FaceRecognitionResult searchFace(FaceRecognitionRequest request);

    LivenessSession createLivenessSession(LivenessSessionRequest request);

}
```

```java id="nilbn4"
public record FaceVerificationRequest(
        UUID jobId,
        UUID referenceAssetId,
        UUID probeAssetId,
        URI referenceImageUri,
        URI probeImageUri,
        FaceThresholdPolicy thresholdPolicy,
        boolean requireLiveness,
        Map<String, Object> options
) {}
```

AWS Rekognition `CompareFaces` provides a similarity score and supports a `QualityFilter` parameter to filter faces below a quality bar; AWS responsible AI docs emphasize face matching is probabilistic, with scores from 0 to 100. ([AWS Documentation](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/rekognition/latest/APIReference/API_CompareFaces.html?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

FFmpeg’s official docs state that ffmpeg will transcode audio, video, and subtitle streams unless `-c copy` is specified, and that stream copy avoids re-encoding when applicable. ([FFmpeg](https://ffmpeg.org/ffmpeg.html?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

## 16. Provider Capability Descriptor

Every adapter should describe what it supports.

```java id="zkwo8c"
        Set<String> supportedInputMimeTypes,
        Set<String> supportedOutputFormats,
        Set<String> supportedLanguages,
        Set<String> supportedRegions,
        boolean supportsSync,

        boolean supportsStreaming,

        boolean supportsWordTimestamps,
        boolean supportsSpeakerDiarization,
        boolean supportsBoundingBoxes,
        boolean supportsTables,
        boolean supportsKeyValuePairs,
        boolean supportsSelectionMarks,
        boolean supportsLiveness,
        Map<String, Object> limits
) {}
```

Example:

```json id="395xu8"
{
  "providerName": "AWS_TEXTRACT",
  "capabilities": ["OCR", "LAYOUT", "TABLES", "KEY_VALUE", "QUERIES"],
  "supportsAsync": true,
  "supportsBoundingBoxes": true,
  "supportsTables": true,
  "supportsKeyValuePairs": true,
  "limits": {
    "maxPages": "provider-policy",
    "inputStorage": "S3"
  }
}
```

## 17. Provider Policy

Provider selection should be data-driven.

```sql id="735tnh"
CREATE TABLE provider_policy (
    id                          UUID PRIMARY KEY,
    policy_key                   VARCHAR(150) NOT NULL,

    tenant_id                   UUID,
    capability                   VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL,
    use_case                     VARCHAR(100),

    primary_provider             VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL,
    fallback_providers           TEXT[],

    required_region              VARCHAR(100),
    data_residency_class          VARCHAR(100),

    max_cost_per_unit             NUMERIC(18,6),
    max_latency_ms                BIGINT,
    min_quality_tier              VARCHAR(50),

    allow_cloud_provider          BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT TRUE,
    allow_on_prem_provider         BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT TRUE,
    allow_fallback_cross_region    BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT FALSE,

    preserve_raw_provider_response BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT TRUE,

    active                      BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT TRUE,
    created_at                  TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
    updated_at                  TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),

    UNIQUE (policy_key)
);
```

Example policies:

| Policy | Capability | Primary | Fallback |
|---|---|---|---|
| `INVOICE_DOC_AI_ID` | Document AI | Azure Document Intelligence | AWS Textract, Tesseract |
| `KYC_FACE_VERIFY` | Face Verification | Azure Face | AWS Rekognition |
| `MEETING_STT_ID` | STT | AWS Transcribe | Google STT |
| `TTS_NOTIFICATION_ID` | TTS | Azure Speech | Google TTS |
| `MEDIA_TRANSCODE_DEFAULT` | Media | Local FFmpeg | none |

## 18. Provider Router

```java id="jrh73s"
public final class ProviderRouter {

    private final ProviderPolicyRepository policyRepository;
    private final ProviderHealthService healthService;
    private final ProviderCostService costService;
    private final ProviderCapabilityRegistry capabilityRegistry;

    public ProviderSelection select(ProviderSelectionRequest request) {
        ProviderPolicy policy = policyRepository.resolve(
                request.tenantId(),
                request.capability(),
                request.useCase()
        );

        List<String> candidates = new ArrayList<>();
        candidates.add(policy.primaryProvider());
        candidates.addAll(policy.fallbackProviders());

        for (String providerName : candidates) {
            ProviderCapabilityDescriptor capability =
                    capabilityRegistry.get(providerName);

            if (!supports(capability, request)) {
                continue;
            }

            if (!healthService.isAvailable(providerName, request.region())) {
                continue;
            }

            if (!costService.withinBudget(providerName, request, policy)) {
                continue;
            }

            if (!dataResidencyAllowed(providerName, request, policy)) {
                continue;
            }

            return ProviderSelection.selected(providerName, policy.policyKey());
        }

        return ProviderSelection.none("NO_PROVIDER_AVAILABLE");
    }
}
```

Selection criteria:

```text id="5hho61"
1. capability support
2. data residency
3. tenant policy
4. provider health
5. cost budget
6. latency SLA
7. quality tier
8. fallback safety
```

---

## 19. Provider Registry

```java id="fkw6ej"
public final class ProviderRegistry<T> {

    private final Map<String, T> providers;

    public ProviderRegistry(List<NamedProvider<T>> providerList) {
        this.providers = providerList.stream()
                .collect(Collectors.toUnmodifiableMap(
                        NamedProvider::name,
                        NamedProvider::provider
                ));
    }

    public T get(String providerName) {
        T provider = providers.get(providerName);

        if (provider == null) {
            throw new ProviderNotFoundException(providerName);
        }

        return provider;
    }
}
```

Example:

```java id="coq2x0"
DocumentAiProvider provider = documentAiProviderRegistry.get(selection.providerName());
DocumentAiResult result = provider.analyze(request);
```

---

## 20. Fallback Strategy

Fallback bukan sekadar “kalau gagal coba provider lain”. Harus dibatasi.

```text id="bxu70w"
Fallback boleh:
- provider timeout
- provider rate limit
- provider temporary outage
- provider region unavailable
- provider returned unsupported transient error

Fallback tidak boleh otomatis:
- consent missing
- policy disallows provider
- data residency violated
- biometric high-impact result gray zone
- provider result says no match
- validation failed due actual bad document
```

Fallback matrix:

| Failure | Fallback? | Reason |
|---|---:|---|
| `PROVIDER_TIMEOUT` | Yes | transient |
| `PROVIDER_RATE_LIMIT` | Yes | capacity |
| `PROVIDER_5XX` | Yes | transient |
| `UNSUPPORTED_FORMAT` | Maybe | fallback may support it |
| `INVALID_INPUT` | No | input problem |
| `CONSENT_MISSING` | No | governance |
| `DATA_RESIDENCY_BLOCKED` | No | compliance |
| `LOW_CONFIDENCE` | Maybe | alternate provider or review |
| `NO_FACE_MATCH` | No auto fallback decision | could become provider shopping |
| `DOCUMENT_VALIDATION_FAILED` | No | business validation issue |

---

## 21. Avoid Provider Shopping

Provider shopping adalah anti-pattern:

```text id="1v23py"
Provider A says face non-match
→ try Provider B
→ Provider B says match
→ accept match
```

Itu berbahaya untuk biometric/high-impact decisions.

Correct approach:

```text id="ze2cln"
If providers disagree:
→ mark REVIEW_REQUIRED
→ preserve both results
→ human/compliance decision if allowed
```

For OCR/Document AI, fallback to improve extraction is usually acceptable. For face matching/identity, fallback must be governed.

---

## 22. Circuit Breaker and Provider Health

Provider adapter harus punya health/rate awareness.

```sql id="7jjudh"
CREATE TABLE provider_health_status (
    id                  UUID PRIMARY KEY,
    provider_name        VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL,
    capability           VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL,
    region               VARCHAR(100),

    status               VARCHAR(50) NOT NULL,
    failure_rate          NUMERIC(8,5),
    p95_latency_ms        BIGINT,

    circuit_state         VARCHAR(50) NOT NULL,
    opened_at             TIMESTAMPTZ,
    half_open_after        TIMESTAMPTZ,

    updated_at            TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),

    UNIQUE (provider_name, capability, region)
);
```

Circuit states:

```text id="9etnqi"
CLOSED
OPEN
HALF_OPEN
FORCED_DISABLED
```

Decision:

```text id="x8qvkg"
if circuit OPEN:
    do not send new jobs except test probes

if HALF_OPEN:
    allow limited probes

if CLOSED:
    normal traffic
```

## 23. Cost Routing

Provider abstraction should include cost estimation.

```java id="7if5nu"
public interface ProviderCostEstimator {

    CostEstimate estimate(ProviderCostRequest request);
}
```

```java id="u8p47s"
public record CostEstimate(
        String providerName,
        String unit,
        BigDecimal estimatedUnits,
        BigDecimal estimatedCost,
        String currency,
        Map<String, Object> details
) {}
```

Cost units:

| Capability | Unit |
|---|---|
| OCR | page |
| Document AI | page / document |
| STT | audio second/minute |
| TTS | character / request / audio duration |
| Face verification | image pair / API call |
| Media processing | CPU-second / video minute / output size |
| On-prem GPU | GPU-second |

Routing examples:

```text id="gek68f"
- Use Tesseract for low-risk searchable text.
- Use Azure/AWS Document AI for invoice extraction.
- Use local FFmpeg for standard transcode.
- Use cloud STT only when language/quality requirement needs it.
- Use on-prem for sensitive tenant if policy requires.
```

---

## 24. Data Residency Routing

```java id="cm5kyt"
public boolean dataResidencyAllowed(
        String providerName,
        ProviderSelectionRequest request,
        ProviderPolicy policy
) {
    ProviderDeployment deployment = providerDeploymentRepository.get(
            providerName,
            request.region()
    );

    if (request.dataClassification().equals("BIOMETRIC")
            && !deployment.allowsBiometric()) {
        return false;
    }

    if (policy.requiredRegion() != null
            && !policy.requiredRegion().equals(deployment.region())) {
        return false;
    }

    if (!policy.allowFallbackCrossRegion()
            && !Objects.equals(request.region(), deployment.region())) {
        return false;
    }

    return true;
}
```

Provider deployment table:

```sql id="f5h3pp"
CREATE TABLE provider_deployment (
    id                      UUID PRIMARY KEY,
    provider_name            VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL,
    capability               VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL,
    region                   VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL,

    endpoint                 TEXT,
    data_residency_class      VARCHAR(100),
    allows_pii               BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT FALSE,
    allows_biometric          BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT FALSE,
    allows_model_training     BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT FALSE,

    active                  BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT TRUE,

    UNIQUE (provider_name, capability, region)
);
```

## 25. Raw Provider Response Storage

Store raw provider result privately.

```text id="8nm6xx"
Why store raw response?
- audit
- debugging
- re-normalization
- migration comparison
- provider support ticket
- regression testing
```

But:

```text id="qe4nay"
- do not expose raw response to client
- store as private media_asset
- apply retention
- redact logs
- classify sensitivity
```

DB pointer:

```sql id="8jfyfl"
ALTER TABLE media_job
ADD COLUMN raw_provider_asset_id UUID REFERENCES media_asset(id);
```

## 27. Adapter Test Fixture

Each adapter needs golden tests.

```text id="aczqs5"
src/test/resources/provider-fixtures/
├── aws-textract/
│   ├── invoice-response.json
│   └── expected-canonical.json
├── azure-document-intelligence/
│   ├── invoice-response.json
│   └── expected-canonical.json
├── google-document-ai/
│   ├── invoice-response.json
│   └── expected-canonical.json
└── tesseract/
    ├── page.tsv
    └── expected-canonical.json
```

Test:

```java id="12v4m2"
@Test
void normalizeAwsTextractInvoice() {
    AwsTextractResponse raw = loadFixture("aws-textract/invoice-response.json");

    CanonicalDocumentAiResult result = normalizer.normalize(raw, context);

    CanonicalDocumentAiResult expected =
            loadExpected("aws-textract/expected-canonical.json");

    assertThat(result).usingRecursiveComparison().isEqualTo(expected);
}
```

## 28. Dual-Run / Shadow Mode

Untuk migration provider, gunakan shadow mode.

```text id="z61qdy"
Primary provider result
→ used by workflow

Shadow provider result
→ computed in background
→ not used for decision
→ compared for quality/cost/latency
```

DB:

```sql id="ok37un"
CREATE TABLE provider_shadow_run (
    id                      UUID PRIMARY KEY,
    job_id                  UUID NOT NULL,

    capability               VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL,
    primary_provider          VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL,
    shadow_provider           VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL,

    primary_result_id         UUID,
    shadow_result_asset_id    UUID,

    comparison_status         VARCHAR(50),
    comparison_json           JSONB,

    created_at               TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
    completed_at             TIMESTAMPTZ
);
```

Use cases:

```text id="bkm57m"
- compare AWS vs Azure OCR quality
- test Google Document AI before migration
- validate on-prem Tesseract fallback
- evaluate new model version
- estimate cost savings
```

## 29. Canonical Error Model

Provider errors must be normalized.

```java id="kwm30r"
public enum ProviderErrorClass {
    PROVIDER_TIMEOUT,
    PROVIDER_RATE_LIMIT,
    PROVIDER_UNAVAILABLE,
    PROVIDER_AUTH_FAILED,
    PROVIDER_QUOTA_EXCEEDED,
    PROVIDER_UNSUPPORTED_INPUT,
    PROVIDER_INVALID_REQUEST,
    PROVIDER_RESULT_PARSE_FAILED,
    PROVIDER_REGION_BLOCKED,
    PROVIDER_POLICY_BLOCKED,
    PROVIDER_DATA_RESIDENCY_BLOCKED,
    PROVIDER_UNKNOWN_ERROR
}
```

Normalized exception:

```java id="e1u0b3"
public final class ProviderException extends RuntimeException {

    private final String providerName;
    private final ProviderErrorClass errorClass;
    private final boolean retryable;
    private final boolean fallbackAllowed;
    private final Map<String, Object> safeMetadata;

    // constructor/getters
}
```

## 30. Provider Error Mapping Table

```sql id="g8mc95"
CREATE TABLE provider_error_mapping (
    id                      UUID PRIMARY KEY,
    provider_name            VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL,
    capability               VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL,

    provider_error_code       VARCHAR(200),
    provider_http_status      INTEGER,

    normalized_error_class    VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL,
    retryable                 BOOLEAN NOT NULL,
    fallback_allowed          BOOLEAN NOT NULL,

    notes                    TEXT,

    UNIQUE (provider_name, capability, provider_error_code, provider_http_status)
);
```

Example:

| Provider Error | Normalized | Retry | Fallback |
|---|---|---:|---:|
| HTTP 429 | `PROVIDER_RATE_LIMIT` | yes | yes |
| HTTP 503 | `PROVIDER_UNAVAILABLE` | yes | yes |
| invalid media | `PROVIDER_UNSUPPORTED_INPUT` | no | maybe |
| auth denied | `PROVIDER_AUTH_FAILED` | no | no |
| region blocked | `PROVIDER_REGION_BLOCKED` | no | no |

## 31. Idempotency Across Providers

Provider retry can create duplicate external jobs.

Use deterministic names where provider supports it:

```text id="avdjdd"
internalJobId = UUID
providerJobName = tenant-usecase-jobId
idempotencyKey = sourceHash + optionsHash + provider + adapterVersion
```

Internal table:

```sql id="jvcapa"
CREATE TABLE provider_job_reference (
    id                      UUID PRIMARY KEY,
    internal_job_id          UUID NOT NULL,

    provider_name            VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL,
    capability               VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL,
    provider_job_id          TEXT,
    provider_job_name        TEXT,

    request_hash             TEXT NOT NULL,
    status                  VARCHAR(50) NOT NULL,

    submitted_at             TIMESTAMPTZ,
    completed_at             TIMESTAMPTZ,

    UNIQUE (provider_name, capability, provider_job_name),
    UNIQUE (internal_job_id, provider_name)
);
```

---

## 32. Async Provider Pattern

Many provider jobs are async.

```text id="q60inf"
Submit provider job
  │
  ▼
store providerJobId
  │
  ▼
poll / callback
  │
  ▼
fetch result
  │
  ▼
normalize
  │
  ▼
persist
```

This fits:

```text id="7vs8fx"
- AWS Transcribe batch
- Textract async document processing
- Azure batch transcription / document analysis LRO
- Google long-running operations
- local GPU job queue
```

Do not block a worker thread for hours. Use Camunda/timer/polling job.

---

## 33. Sync Provider Pattern

Some operations can be sync:

```text id="ts71cx"
- small OCR image
- face detection
- face verification
- short TTS
- thumbnail generation
```

Even sync operations should run in worker if:

```text id="ycw7sb"
- input file large
- provider latency unpredictable
- operation expensive
- result needs durable audit
```

Jersey resource should remain thin.

---

## 34. Adapter Observability

Instrument all provider calls.

OpenTelemetry is a vendor-neutral observability framework for generating, collecting, and exporting telemetry such as traces, metrics, and logs; it supports correlation across services/components. ([OpenTelemetry](https://opentelemetry.io/docs/?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

Required metrics:

| Metric | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `provider_request_total` | calls by provider/capability |
| `provider_success_total` | success |
| `provider_error_total` | failure by class |
| `provider_latency_seconds` | latency |
| `provider_retry_total` | retries |
| `provider_fallback_total` | fallback usage |
| `provider_circuit_open_total` | circuit breaker |
| `provider_cost_estimate` | cost |
| `provider_result_quality_score` | quality metric |
| `provider_shadow_diff_total` | migration mismatch |
| `provider_rate_limited_total` | quota issue |

Trace attributes:

```text id="ph99i5"
provider.name
provider.capability
provider.region
provider.request_id
provider.model_name
provider.model_version
adapter.version
normalizer.version
tenant.id
job.id
fallback.used
```

---

```text id="5iil8i"
PROVIDER_SELECTED
PROVIDER_REQUEST_SUBMITTED

PROVIDER_FALLBACK_STARTED
```

## 36. Provider Config and Secrets

Do not store provider secrets in DB.

```text id="yj4j77"
Provider config:
- region
- endpoint
- model ID
- feature flags
- timeout
- retry policy

Secrets:
- API key
- client secret
- access token
- private key
```

Secrets should be in:

```text id="vsr65h"
- Kubernetes Secret
- cloud secret manager
- vault
- workload identity / IAM role preferred
```

DB may store secret reference only:

```sql id="qv8lkv"
CREATE TABLE provider_config (
    id                  UUID PRIMARY KEY,
    provider_name        VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL,
    capability           VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL,
    region               VARCHAR(100),

    endpoint             TEXT,
    model_id             TEXT,
    secret_ref           TEXT,

    timeout_ms           BIGINT NOT NULL,
    max_retries           INTEGER NOT NULL,

    active              BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT TRUE,

    UNIQUE (provider_name, capability, region)
);
```

## 37. Java Adapter Skeleton - Document AI

```java id="evi5ul"
public final class AwsTextractDocumentAiProvider implements DocumentAiProvider {

    private final TextractClient textractClient;
    private final AwsTextractNormalizer normalizer;
    private final RawProviderResultStore rawStore;

    @Override
    public DocumentAiResult analyze(DocumentAiRequest request) {
        try {
            AwsTextractRawResult raw = callTextract(request);

            UUID rawAssetId = request.preserveRawProviderResponse()
                    ? rawStore.store(request.jobId(), "AWS_TEXTRACT", raw)
                    : null;

            DocumentAiResult canonical = normalizer.normalize(raw, request);

            return canonical.withRawProviderAssetId(rawAssetId);

        } catch (TextractException e) {
            throw mapException(e);
        }
    }

    private AwsTextractRawResult callTextract(DocumentAiRequest request) {
        // Build provider-specific request here.
        // Keep AWS SDK objects inside adapter.
        throw new UnsupportedOperationException("Skeleton");
    }

    private ProviderException mapException(Exception e) {
        // Map provider-specific exception to canonical ProviderException.
        throw new UnsupportedOperationException("Skeleton");
    }

    @Override
    public ProviderCapabilityDescriptor capabilities() {
        return AwsTextractCapabilities.DESCRIPTOR;
    }
}
```

---

## 38. Java Adapter Skeleton - Local FFmpeg

```java id="a1colb"
public final class LocalFfmpegMediaProcessingProvider implements MediaProcessingProvider {

    private final FfmpegCommandFactory commandFactory;
    private final FfmpegRunner runner;
    private final FfprobeService ffprobeService;
    private final ObjectStorageClient storageClient;

    @Override
    public MediaProcessingResult process(MediaProcessingRequest request) {
        Path workspace = createWorkspace(request.jobId());

        try {
            Path input = storageClient.downloadToWorkspace(request.sourceUri(), workspace);

            MediaProbeResult inputProbe = ffprobeService.probe(input, Duration.ofSeconds(30));

            Path output = workspace.resolve(resolveOutputName(request));

            List<String> args = commandFactory.build(request, input, output, inputProbe);

            FfmpegRunResult run = runner.run(args, resolveTimeout(request));

            if (!run.success()) {
                throw mapFfmpegFailure(run);
            }

            MediaProbeResult outputProbe = ffprobeService.probe(output, Duration.ofSeconds(30));

            UUID outputAssetId = storageClient.uploadDerivedMedia(request.jobId(), output, outputProbe);

            return MediaProcessingResult.completed(
                    request.jobId(),
                    outputAssetId,
                    ProviderMetadata.local("LOCAL_FFMPEG", run.ffmpegVersion())
            );

        } finally {
            cleanup(workspace);
        }
    }
}
```

---

## 39. Canonical Result Quality

Each capability needs quality summary.

| Capability | Quality Signals |
|---|---|
| OCR | mean confidence, word count, empty page count |
| Document AI | required field coverage, validation status, table consistency |
| STT | mean confidence, low-confidence segment count, word timestamps |
| TTS | generation success, audio duration, speech mark presence |
| Face | quality gate, liveness, similarity, threshold band |
| Media | output validation, duration match, resolution, codec compatibility |

Canonical quality object:

```java id="q0l59k"
public record ResultQualitySummary(
        double confidence,
        String confidenceBand,
        boolean requiresReview,
        List<String> qualityFlags,
        Map<String, Object> metrics
) {}
```

## 40. Provider Migration Plan

When migrating providers:

```text id="g1j1ai"
1. Build new adapter
2. Build normalizer
3. Add capability descriptor
4. Add provider policy disabled
5. Run golden fixture tests
6. Run shadow mode on production-like traffic
7. Compare quality/cost/latency
8. Tune threshold/schema if needed
9. Enable small percentage
10. Monitor metrics
11. Rollout gradually
12. Keep rollback path
```

Do not do:

```text id="l226a7"
Change provider in config Friday evening and hope result shape stays same.
```

---

## 44. Security Checklist

```text id="ea25u7"
[ ] provider secrets not in DB
[ ] raw provider response private
[ ] provider access scoped by IAM/managed identity
[ ] data residency rules enforced before call
[ ] biometric provider calls require purpose/consent policy
[ ] no provider shopping for identity decisions
[ ] signed URLs short-lived
[ ] provider request IDs logged safely
[ ] retries do not duplicate external jobs
[ ] fallback does not bypass policy
```

---

## 45. Common Anti-Patterns

| Anti-pattern | Dampak | Alternatif |
|---|---|---|
| One giant `AiProvider` | interface kacau | capability-specific interfaces |
| Return raw provider response to client | vendor lock-in | canonical result |
| No raw result storage | audit/debug sulit | private raw artifact |
| Fallback on all errors | policy/decision risk | fallback matrix |
| Provider shopping for face match | false accept risk | review on disagreement |
| Cost ignored | budget surprise | cost estimator |
| Region ignored | compliance risk | data residency policy |
| No normalizer version | result drift | versioned normalizer |
| No golden tests | adapter regression | fixture-based tests |
| Hard-code provider in service | migration costly | provider router |
| Use provider score as universal score | wrong threshold | provider-specific calibration |
| No circuit breaker | cascading failure | provider health/circuit |

---

```text id="az2ok4"
1. Define canonical interfaces:
   - OcrProvider

   - SpeechToTextProvider
   - TextToSpeechProvider

4. Add:
   - raw provider result storage
   - provider metadata
   - adapter/normalizer version
```

```text id="6spx67"
+ no automatic fallback initially

+ canonical normalizer
```

Saya lanjut ke **Seri 20/20**: production hardening. Ini seri penutup yang menyatukan semua pipeline OCR, speech, face, document AI, TTS, dan media processing menjadi sistem yang bisa dioperasikan: observability, queue lag, retry, cost, Kubernetes/GPU, SLO, incident, dan capacity planning.

# Seri 20/20 - Production Hardening  
## Observability, Cost Control, Queue Lag, GPU/Kubernetes, Failure Handling, SLO, Incident Playbook, Capacity Planning, dan Operational Dashboard

## 1. Introduction

Seri terakhir ini menyatukan semua capability yang sudah kita bahas:

```text id="jb6s56"
- OCR
- Layout-aware OCR
- Document AI
- Human review
- Speech-to-Text batch
- Streaming transcription
- Diarization/subtitle
- Text-to-Speech
- FFmpeg media processing
- Face detection/verification/recognition
- WebAuthn/passkey boundary
- Biometric governance
- Provider abstraction
```

Production hardening berarti sistem tidak hanya “jalan di laptop”, tetapi bisa:

```text id="ji658s"
- dipantau
- diskalakan
- dikontrol cost-nya
- gagal dengan aman
- retry dengan benar
- tidak kehilangan job/result
- punya audit trail
- punya SLO
- punya incident playbook
- siap untuk Kubernetes/on-prem/cloud
```

Untuk workload media/AI, production hardening lebih kompleks daripada REST CRUD biasa karena job bisa besar, lama, mahal, stateful, dan privacy-sensitive.

---

## 2. Mental Model

```text id="22jhr3"
Media/AI Production System
=
API control plane
+ async job execution
+ object storage
+ workflow orchestration
+ provider abstraction
+ observability
+ cost guardrail
+ retry/failure model
+ security/governance
+ operational dashboard
```

Prinsip utama:

```text id="6aog3w"
Jersey API harus tetap ringan.
Worker harus scalable dan isolated.
Database harus menjadi source of truth untuk state.
Object storage harus menjadi source of truth untuk binary artifacts.
Observability harus menjawab “apa yang rusak, di mana, kenapa, dan dampaknya apa?”
```

OpenTelemetry menyediakan framework observability vendor-neutral untuk telemetry seperti traces, metrics, logs, dan collector; semantic conventions membantu memberi nama atribut yang konsisten untuk traces, metrics, logs, profiles, dan resources. Ini penting supaya semua service, worker, dan provider adapter punya telemetry yang bisa dikorelasikan. ([OpenTelemetry](https://opentelemetry.io/?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

```text id="xbr7tb"
        ├── submit job

Queue / Camunda / Worker Claim Loop

Provider Layer

Observability + Ops
```
