## 4. Production Hardening Skill Map

| Area | Fokus | Kenapa penting |
|---|---|---|
| Job lifecycle | state machine, retry, cancellation, poison job | mencegah job stuck/hilang |
| Queue lag | backlog, worker throughput, SLA | indikator utama kesehatan async system |
| Observability | metrics, logs, traces, audit | debugging dan incident |
| SLO/SLI | latency, success rate, review SLA | ekspektasi operasional |
| Cost control | provider cost, duration, pages, chars, GPU | mencegah tagihan meledak |
| Capacity planning | CPU, memory, disk, GPU, provider quota | readiness skala |
| Kubernetes hardening | requests/limits/probes/ephemeral storage | stabilitas deployment |
| GPU scheduling | device plugin, node pool, resource isolation | inference/media berat |
| Failure model | retryable/permanent/user error/provider error | keputusan retry aman |
| Data lifecycle | retention, cleanup, legal hold | privacy/storage |
| Security | signed URL, private storage, secrets, IAM | data/media sensitif |
| Provider reliability | circuit breaker, fallback, timeout | isolasi vendor outage |
| Human ops | dashboard, runbook, incident drill | operability nyata |
| Release safety | canary, shadow mode, feature flag | migration aman |

---

## 5. Control Plane vs Execution Plane

Pisahkan dua plane:

```text id="jz9hz6"
Control Plane:
- Jersey API
- auth/authz
- submit job
- status/result
- admin retry/cancel
- review API

Execution Plane:
- workers
- FFmpeg
- OCR engine
- STT/TTS provider call
- face model/provider
- document AI adapter
- retention/deletion worker
```

Rule:

```text id="gec21c"
Control Plane harus cepat, stateless, dan resilient.
Execution Plane boleh berat, async, isolated, dan autoscaled.
```

Anti-pattern:

```text id="449wfx"
POST /ocr
→ upload PDF 200 halaman
→ Jersey thread OCR langsung
→ request timeout
→ pod memory naik
→ user retry
→ duplicate cost
```

Correct pattern:

```text id="wiqz2p"
POST /ocr-jobs
→ validate
→ create job
→ return jobId
→ worker process async
→ GET status/result
```

---

## 6. Core Production State Machine

Gunakan state machine konsisten lintas capability.

```text id="9brdfq"
SUBMITTED
  │
  ▼
VALIDATING
  │
  ├── rejected/security → REJECTED / SECURITY_REJECTED
  ▼
STAGED
  │
  ▼
PROCESSING
  │
  ├── transient failure → RETRY_PENDING
  ├── permanent failure → FAILED
  ├── low confidence    → NEEDS_REVIEW
  ├── cancel requested  → CANCEL_REQUESTED
  ▼
RESULT_READY
  │
  ▼
COMPLETED
  │
  ▼
ARCHIVED / EXPIRED
```

Tambahkan states untuk provider async:

```text id="7mffcc"
PROVIDER_SUBMITTED
PROVIDER_RUNNING
PROVIDER_RESULT_AVAILABLE
PROVIDER_RESULT_FETCHING
RESULT_NORMALIZING
```

---

## 7. Database State Invariants

Invariants yang harus selalu benar:

```text id="5jo3c0"
COMPLETED job harus punya result.
FAILED job harus punya error_class.
PROCESSING job harus punya locked_by / worker lease.
RETRY_PENDING job harus punya next_attempt_at.
NEEDS_REVIEW job harus punya review reason/task.
CANCELLED job tidak boleh diproses worker baru.
EXPIRED asset tidak boleh dipakai job baru.
```

Example constraint-ish checks:

```sql id="l8b468"
ALTER TABLE media_job
ADD CONSTRAINT chk_failed_has_error
CHECK (
    status != 'FAILED'
    OR error_class IS NOT NULL
);
```

```sql id="3dkdcx"
ALTER TABLE media_job
ADD CONSTRAINT chk_retry_has_next_attempt
CHECK (
    status != 'RETRY_PENDING'
    OR next_attempt_at IS NOT NULL
);
```

---

## 8. Worker Claiming

Untuk PostgreSQL-backed queue, gunakan claim dengan lock/lease.

```sql id="p8p7q5"
WITH candidate AS (
    SELECT id
    FROM media_job
    WHERE status IN ('SUBMITTED', 'RETRY_PENDING')
      AND (next_attempt_at IS NULL OR next_attempt_at <= now())
      AND job_type = :jobType
    ORDER BY priority DESC, created_at ASC
    FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED
    LIMIT 1
)
UPDATE media_job j
SET status = 'PROCESSING',
    locked_by = :workerId,
    locked_until = now() + interval '10 minutes',
    attempt_count = attempt_count + 1,
    updated_at = now()
FROM candidate
WHERE j.id = candidate.id
RETURNING j.*;
```

Why lease matters:

```text id="gr1l1n"
Jika worker pod mati, job tidak hilang.
Setelah locked_until lewat, job bisa direclaim.
```

---

## 9. Queue Lag

Queue lag adalah sinyal operasional utama untuk async pipeline.

```text id="xjmegp"
queue lag = now - created_at job tertua yang belum diproses
```

Metrics:

```text id="8g2q95"
media_job_queue_depth{job_type,status}
media_job_oldest_age_seconds{job_type}
media_job_processing_duration_seconds{job_type}
media_job_attempt_count{job_type}
media_job_retry_pending_total{job_type}
media_job_poison_total{job_type}
```

Query:

```sql id="ia0zp9"
SELECT
    job_type,
    status,
    COUNT(*) AS depth,
    EXTRACT(EPOCH FROM now() - MIN(created_at)) AS oldest_age_seconds
FROM media_job
WHERE status IN ('SUBMITTED', 'RETRY_PENDING', 'PROCESSING', 'NEEDS_REVIEW')
GROUP BY job_type, status;
```

KEDA dapat menskalakan workload Kubernetes berdasarkan jumlah event yang perlu diproses, dan dokumentasi ScaledJob menyebut konsep queue length serta target average value untuk menentukan jumlah job yang dibuat. Ini relevan untuk worker yang dipicu oleh queue depth/lag. ([KEDA](https://keda.sh/?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

## 10. Worker Pool Separation

Jangan satu worker mengerjakan semua.

```text id="gg1scv"
Recommended workers:
- upload-validation-worker
- metadata-worker
- ocr-worker
- document-ai-worker
- stt-worker
- streaming-gateway
- tts-worker
- ffmpeg-worker
- face-worker
- review-worker
- retention-worker
- callback/webhook-worker
```

Kenapa?

| Worker | Resource Profile |
|---|---|
| OCR | CPU-heavy, memory medium |
| FFmpeg transcode | CPU/disk-heavy, memory medium |
| STT cloud submit/poll | network/provider-bound |
| TTS | provider-bound, cache-sensitive |
| Face local model | GPU/CPU-heavy |
| Retention | storage/API-bound |
| Review | DB/API-bound |
| Streaming gateway | network/connection-bound |

---

## 11. Kubernetes Resource Hardening

Untuk worker berat, selalu set:

```text id="bnlfa1"
- CPU requests/limits
- memory requests/limits
- ephemeral-storage requests/limits
- liveness/readiness/startup probes
- graceful shutdown
- PodDisruptionBudget where needed
```

Kubernetes scheduler memakai resource requests untuk menempatkan Pod, sedangkan limit membatasi penggunaan resource container; dokumentasi Kubernetes juga menjelaskan memory request sebagai jumlah memory yang dijamin dan memory limit sebagai batas yang tidak boleh dilampaui. ([Kubernetes](https://v1-32.docs.kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/configuration/manage-resources-containers/?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

Example worker deployment:

```yaml id="tksbzs"
apiVersion: apps/v1
kind: Deployment
metadata:
  name: ocr-worker
spec:
  replicas: 3
  selector:
    matchLabels:
      app: ocr-worker
  template:
    metadata:
      labels:
        app: ocr-worker
    spec:
      terminationGracePeriodSeconds: 60
      containers:
        - name: ocr-worker
          image: company/ocr-worker:1.0.0
          resources:
            requests:
              cpu: "1000m"
              memory: "1Gi"
              ephemeral-storage: "2Gi"
            limits:
              cpu: "4000m"
              memory: "4Gi"
              ephemeral-storage: "10Gi"
          readinessProbe:
            httpGet:
              path: /health/ready
              port: 8080
            periodSeconds: 10
          livenessProbe:
            httpGet:
              path: /health/live
              port: 8080
            periodSeconds: 30
          startupProbe:
            httpGet:
              path: /health/startup
              port: 8080
            failureThreshold: 30
            periodSeconds: 10
```

Kubernetes probes dapat dipakai untuk memonitor health container; berdasarkan hasil probe, Kubernetes dapat restart container yang tidak sehat atau menghentikan pengiriman traffic ke container yang belum ready. ([Kubernetes](https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/workloads/pods/probes/?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

## 12. Ephemeral Storage

FFmpeg, OCR preprocessing, PDF rasterization, dan frame extraction bisa memakan temporary disk besar.

Kubernetes mendukung request/limit untuk `ephemeral-storage`, dan scheduler mempertimbangkan request tersebut saat menempatkan Pod; dokumentasi Kubernetes juga menunjukkan `emptyDir` dapat mengonsumsi bagian dari limit ephemeral storage Pod. ([Kubernetes](https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/storage/ephemeral-storage/?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

Practical rules:

```text id="8e34xk"
- setiap job punya workspace sendiri
- temp file dibersihkan di finally block
- output diverifikasi lalu upload ke object storage
- jangan simpan artifact final di temp disk
- monitor temp disk usage
- batasi frame extraction
```

Example:

```yaml id="8zlveg"
resources:
  requests:
    ephemeral-storage: "5Gi"
  limits:
    ephemeral-storage: "20Gi"

volumeMounts:
  - name: media-tmp
    mountPath: /tmp/media-worker

volumes:
  - name: media-tmp
    emptyDir:
      sizeLimit: 20Gi
```

---

## 13. GPU / Accelerator Scheduling

Untuk face embedding local, OCR model, ASR model, atau TTS local, GPU bisa dibutuhkan.

Kubernetes device plugin framework digunakan untuk mengiklankan hardware khusus seperti GPU, NIC, FPGA, atau non-volatile memory ke kubelet; Kubernetes juga mendokumentasikan scheduling GPU via resource limits, di mana GPU biasanya dispesifikasikan sebagai limit resource vendor-specific. ([Kubernetes](https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/extend-kubernetes/compute-storage-net/device-plugins/?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

Example conceptual:

```yaml id="hbextq"
apiVersion: apps/v1
kind: Deployment
metadata:
  name: face-embedding-worker
spec:
  replicas: 2
  template:
    spec:
      nodeSelector:
        accelerator: nvidia
      containers:
        - name: face-worker
          image: company/face-worker-gpu:1.0.0
          resources:
            limits:
              nvidia.com/gpu: 1
              memory: "8Gi"
              cpu: "4"
```

GPU hardening checklist:

```text id="k9g4ru"
[ ] separate GPU node pool
[ ] device plugin installed
[ ] model warmup strategy
[ ] max concurrent inferences per GPU
[ ] queue per model
[ ] fallback CPU/provider policy
[ ] GPU memory metrics
[ ] model version pinned
[ ] graceful drain before rollout
```

---

## 14. Graceful Shutdown

Worker harus bisa berhenti tanpa merusak job.

```text id="9s4x6s"
SIGTERM received
  │
  ▼
stop claiming new jobs
  │
  ▼
finish current safe step or checkpoint
  │
  ├── if can finish quickly → complete
  ├── if long-running → release lease / mark retry
  └── if external provider job submitted → persist provider_job_id
  │
  ▼
exit
```

Checklist:

```text id="dc5fsp"
[ ] stop polling new jobs
[ ] persist in-flight provider_job_id
[ ] cleanup temp workspace
[ ] release or let lease expire
[ ] do not mark FAILED just because shutdown
[ ] Kubernetes terminationGracePeriodSeconds sufficient
```

---

## 15. Retry Model

Retry harus berdasarkan classification.

```text id="p881za"
Retryable:
- provider timeout
- provider 5xx
- provider rate limit
- object storage transient
- worker pod killed
- DB deadlock/serialization transient

Non-retryable:
- invalid MIME
- corrupt media
- unsupported codec
- no face detected
- consent missing
- policy rejected
- file too large
```

Retry schedule:

```text id="clcc59"
attempt 1 → immediate / 30s
attempt 2 → 2 minutes
attempt 3 → 10 minutes
attempt 4 → 30 minutes
attempt 5 → poison / incident / manual retry
```

DB fields:

```sql id="zbw1m2"
ALTER TABLE media_job
ADD COLUMN attempt_count INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
ADD COLUMN max_attempts INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 5,
ADD COLUMN next_attempt_at TIMESTAMPTZ,
ADD COLUMN last_failure_class VARCHAR(100),
ADD COLUMN poison_reason TEXT;
```

---

## 16. Poison Job

Poison job adalah job yang selalu gagal dan menghabiskan kapasitas.

```text id="sid1wk"
Transient failure → retry
Repeated failure beyond max attempts → POISONED
```

Status:

```text id="mco48s"
POISONED
```

Actions:

```text id="34sxgw"
- stop automatic retry
- preserve error details
- emit alert if volume high
- allow admin/manual retry after fix
- attach run history
```

Table:

```sql id="0m3xtp"
CREATE TABLE media_job_failure_history (
    id                  UUID PRIMARY KEY,
    job_id              UUID NOT NULL REFERENCES media_job(id),

    attempt_number       INTEGER NOT NULL,
    failure_class        VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL,
    failure_code         VARCHAR(100),
    failure_message      TEXT,
    retryable            BOOLEAN NOT NULL,

    worker_id            TEXT,
    provider_name        VARCHAR(100),
    occurred_at          TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now()
);
```

---

## 17. Timeout Budget

Set timeout per stage, bukan satu timeout global.

```text id="drkdic"
Upload finalize: 30s
Metadata extraction: 1-2 min
PDF rasterization: proportional to pages
OCR per page: proportional
Provider submit: 30s
Provider async running: based on media duration/pages
Result fetch: 1-5 min
FFmpeg transcode: proportional to media duration
```

Timeout table:

| Capability | Timeout Basis |
|---|---|
| OCR image | image size/megapixels |
| OCR PDF | page count × per-page budget |
| STT batch | audio duration/provider SLA |
| TTS | char count/chunk count |
| FFmpeg transcode | media duration × factor |
| Face verification | short sync timeout |
| Provider async poll | max provider running time |
| Review task | SLA due date, not technical timeout |

---

## 18. SLO / SLI Design

Production needs SLOs.

Example SLIs:

```text id="5iaszb"
Availability:
- successful API submit rate
- status/result API availability

Latency:
- p95 job submit latency
- p95 OCR completion time
- p95 STT completion time
- p95 TTS completion time
- p95 thumbnail generation time

Correctness / Quality:
- result persistence success rate
- review correction rate
- provider parse failure rate

Freshness:
- queue lag
- oldest unprocessed job age
- review backlog age
```

Example SLOs:

| Service | SLO |
|---|---|
| Job submit API | 99.9% successful under valid input |
| Status API | p95 < 300 ms |
| Thumbnail | 95% complete < 2 minutes |
| OCR image | 95% complete < 5 minutes |
| STT 30-min audio | 90% complete < 45 minutes |
| Human review normal | 95% complete within 1 business day |
| Retention deletion | 99% deleted within policy window |

Prometheus alerting guidance recommends keeping alerts simple, alerting on symptoms, having good consoles to investigate causes, and avoiding pages where there is no operator action. ([Prometheus](https://prometheus.io/docs/practices/alerting/?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

## 19. Metrics Taxonomy

Gunakan empat level metrics:

```text id="iqdjgd"
1. API metrics
2. Job/workflow metrics
3. Provider metrics
4. Business/quality metrics
```

### API metrics

```text id="k7osrg"
http_server_requests_seconds
http_requests_total
api_validation_failed_total
api_idempotency_hit_total
api_authz_denied_total
```

### Job metrics

```text id="kzkglg"
media_job_created_total
media_job_completed_total
media_job_failed_total
media_job_queue_depth
media_job_oldest_age_seconds
media_job_processing_duration_seconds
media_job_retry_total
media_job_poison_total
```

### Provider metrics

```text id="nqva6r"
provider_request_total
provider_latency_seconds
provider_error_total
provider_rate_limited_total
provider_fallback_total
provider_cost_estimate
provider_circuit_state
```

### Quality metrics

```text id="xucoo4"
ocr_mean_confidence
document_ai_review_required_total
transcription_low_confidence_segment_total
face_liveness_failed_total
face_gray_zone_total
tts_cache_hit_total
ffmpeg_output_validation_failed_total
```

Prometheus supports recording rules and alerting rules evaluated at intervals, which is useful for precomputing queue-lag/cost/SLO indicators and triggering actionable alerts. ([Prometheus](https://prometheus.io/docs/prometheus/latest/configuration/recording_rules/?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

## 20. Histogram Strategy

Use histograms for latency/duration.

Examples:

```text id="w7uycv"
media_job_processing_duration_seconds_bucket{job_type}
provider_latency_seconds_bucket{provider,capability}
ffmpeg_run_duration_seconds_bucket{profile}
ocr_page_duration_seconds_bucket{document_type}
```

Prometheus histograms and summaries are specialized metric types for distributions; histograms are commonly used for latency and duration because they can be aggregated and used for percentile estimation. ([Prometheus](https://prometheus.io/docs/practices/histograms/?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

PromQL example:

```promql id="nra4ww"
histogram_quantile(
  0.95,
  sum(rate(media_job_processing_duration_seconds_bucket[5m]))
  by (le, job_type)
)
```

---

## 21. Logs

Logs harus structured.

```json id="eekjwy"
{
  "timestamp": "2026-07-13T22:10:00+07:00",
  "level": "INFO",
  "message": "OCR job completed",
  "correlationId": "corr_123",
  "jobId": "job_456",
  "tenantId": "tnt_001",
  "caseId": "CASE-001",
  "jobType": "OCR_PDF",
  "provider": "TESSERACT",
  "durationMs": 84200,
  "pageCount": 12,
  "meanConfidence": 0.91
}
```

Never log:

```text id="ty4pxf"
- full OCR text
- transcript text
- face image URL with token
- embedding/vector
- raw provider response
- signed URL
- API key/token
- full ID document data
```

Log only safe metadata.

## 22. Tracing

Trace end-to-end job flow.

```text id="lmeqty"
POST /ocr-jobs
  │ traceId
  ▼
DB create job
  │
  ▼
Worker claim
  │
  ▼
Object storage download
  │
  ▼
Preprocess
  │
  ▼
Provider call
  │
  ▼
Normalize result
  │
  ▼
DB persist
  │
  ▼
Webhook callback
```

Recommended span attributes:

```text id="ow7j6c"
job.id
job.type
tenant.id
case.id
asset.id
provider.name
provider.region
provider.request_id
model.name
model.version
adapter.version
normalizer.version
retry.attempt
error.class
```

OpenTelemetry semantic conventions standardize common attribute names across telemetry signals; this is useful to keep service-level, provider-level, and worker-level spans queryable in a consistent way. ([OpenTelemetry](https://opentelemetry.io/docs/concepts/semantic-conventions/?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

## 23. Correlation IDs

Every request/job/event should carry correlation.

```text id="pmv55y"
correlationId
requestId
jobId
assetId
caseId
tenantId
providerRequestId
camundaProcessInstanceId
```

Rules:

```text id="qmuwo6"
- API creates or accepts correlationId
- job stores correlationId
- worker logs correlationId
- provider adapter logs providerRequestId
- audit event stores correlationId
- webhook includes correlationId
```

---

## 24. Audit vs Logs

Audit is not the same as logs.

| Aspect | Logs | Audit |
|---|---|---|
| Purpose | debugging/ops | accountability/compliance |
| Retention | shorter | longer/policy |
| Mutability | log system | append-only preferred |
| Content | technical event | who did what, why |
| Access | engineering | security/compliance-limited |
| PII | minimize | controlled if necessary |

Audit events:

```text id="082b4i"
JOB_CREATED
ASSET_UPLOADED
PROVIDER_SELECTED
RESULT_PERSISTED
REVIEW_REQUIRED
REVIEW_COMPLETED
FIELD_CORRECTED
BIOMETRIC_CONSENT_CHECKED
BIOMETRIC_ARTIFACT_DELETED
ADMIN_RETRY_TRIGGERED
```

---

## 25. Cost Control

Cost dimensions per capability:

| Capability | Cost Driver |
|---|---|
| OCR | pages/images |
| Document AI | pages/document type/features |
| STT | audio duration |
| Streaming STT | stream duration/concurrency |
| TTS | characters/audio duration |
| Face | API calls/images/liveness sessions |
| FFmpeg | CPU/GPU seconds, storage |
| Object storage | GB-month, requests, egress |
| DB | storage/index/write volume |
| Review | human review time |

Cost guardrails:

```text id="1b2g40"
- max pages per job
- max duration per audio/video
- max file size
- max concurrent jobs per tenant
- max monthly quota per tenant
- provider budget
- cache TTS
- reuse normalized audio
- avoid duplicate job via idempotency
```

---

## 26. Cost Estimate Table

```sql id="5r00qz"
CREATE TABLE media_job_cost_estimate (
    id                  UUID PRIMARY KEY,
    job_id              UUID NOT NULL REFERENCES media_job(id),

    capability           VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL,
    provider_name         VARCHAR(100),

    unit_name             VARCHAR(50) NOT NULL,
    estimated_units       NUMERIC(18,6) NOT NULL,
    estimated_cost        NUMERIC(18,6),
    currency              VARCHAR(10),

    cost_policy_key       VARCHAR(150),
    calculated_at         TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),

    metadata_json         JSONB
);
```

Example metadata:

```json id="z9p31h"
{
  "pageCount": 120,
  "documentType": "INVOICE",
  "provider": "AZURE_DOCUMENT_INTELLIGENCE",
  "features": ["layout", "keyValue", "tables"]
}
```

---

## 27. Quota Policy

```sql id="a399wf"
CREATE TABLE tenant_media_quota_policy (
    id                      UUID PRIMARY KEY,
    tenant_id               UUID NOT NULL,

    capability               VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL,

    max_jobs_per_day          INTEGER,
    max_pages_per_day         INTEGER,
    max_audio_minutes_per_day INTEGER,
    max_video_minutes_per_day INTEGER,
    max_tts_chars_per_day     BIGINT,
    max_cost_per_month        NUMERIC(18,6),

    created_at               TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
    updated_at               TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),

    UNIQUE (tenant_id, capability)
);
```

Quota behavior:

```text id="l6deou"
- soft limit → warning/admin alert
- hard limit → reject/queue/manual approval
- burst limit → rate limit
- budget exceeded → switch cheaper provider if policy allows
```

---

## 28. Backpressure

Backpressure prevents overload.

Signals:

```text id="yn274t"
- queue lag high
- worker CPU high
- provider rate limit
- DB connection pool exhausted
- object storage errors
- temp disk pressure
- review backlog high
```

Actions:

```text id="idgsn9"
- reject new large jobs temporarily
- lower max concurrent workers
- pause provider
- switch provider
- require manual approval for costly jobs
- degrade to cheaper/lower-quality profile
- disable non-critical features
```

Example API response:

```json id="6yr4j6"
{
  "error": "SYSTEM_BUSY",
  "message": "Media processing queue is currently above operational threshold. Try again later.",
  "retryAfterSeconds": 300
}
```

---

## 29. Rate Limiting

Rate limit by:

```text id="yj0wbo"
- tenant
- user
- API key/client
- capability
- file size/duration
- provider cost estimate
```

Example limits:

| Capability | Limit |
|---|---|
| upload sessions | 100/hour/tenant |
| OCR pages | 10,000/day/tenant |
| STT minutes | 2,000/day/tenant |
| TTS chars | 1,000,000/day/tenant |
| Face verification | 5,000/day/tenant |
| streaming sessions | 50 concurrent/tenant |
| FFmpeg transcode | 10 concurrent/tenant |

---

## 30. Provider Circuit Breaker

Provider outage must not cascade.

Circuit states:

```text id="86hw3j"
CLOSED → normal
OPEN → provider disabled temporarily
HALF_OPEN → limited test traffic
FORCED_DISABLED → operator disabled
```

Circuit triggers:

```text id="8cg6i6"
- error rate above threshold
- p95 latency above threshold
- 429 rate limit storm
- auth failure
- provider incident
```

Fallback policy:

```text id="i6flg9"
OPEN primary provider
  │
  ├── fallback allowed → switch
  ├── fallback not allowed → queue/retry
  └── high-risk identity → review/manual, no provider shopping
```

---

## 31. Webhook / Callback Hardening

For async completion callbacks:

```text id="tu4j9j"
- sign webhook payload
- include idempotency/event ID
- retry with exponential backoff
- store delivery attempt
- avoid sending sensitive data
- send result pointer, not full result
```

Table:

```sql id="1pf4e5"
CREATE TABLE webhook_delivery (
    id                  UUID PRIMARY KEY,
    job_id              UUID NOT NULL REFERENCES media_job(id),

    target_url_hash      TEXT NOT NULL,
    event_type           VARCHAR(100) NOT NULL,
    event_id             UUID NOT NULL,

    status              VARCHAR(50) NOT NULL,
    attempt_count        INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
    next_attempt_at      TIMESTAMPTZ,

    last_response_code   INTEGER,
    last_error_message   TEXT,

    created_at           TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
    updated_at           TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),

    UNIQUE (event_id, target_url_hash)
);
```

---

## 32. Result Consistency

Result persistence must be atomic.

```text id="b9f3jr"
Persist result
  │
  ├── insert result row
  ├── insert segments/fields/pages
  ├── insert output asset pointer
  ├── update job status
  └── insert audit event
```

Recommended transaction:

```text id="dcuct2"
DB transaction:
- insert canonical result
- insert hot rows
- update job status RESULT_READY/COMPLETED
- insert job_event

Object storage upload should happen before DB pointer is finalized,
but DB should verify checksum/asset metadata.
```

---

## 33. Idempotency

Idempotency across layers:

```text id="yv1cwh"
API idempotency key
Job unique idempotency key
Provider job reference
Result unique by job_id
Webhook event unique
Review completion idempotency key
Artifact cache key
```

Example:

```sql id="rjhecr"
CREATE UNIQUE INDEX uq_media_job_idempotency
ON media_job (tenant_id, idempotency_key)
WHERE idempotency_key IS NOT NULL;
```

```sql id="fucgjp"
CREATE UNIQUE INDEX uq_ocr_result_job
ON ocr_result (job_id);
```

---

## 34. Data Retention and Cleanup

Retention workers should handle:

```text id="j4rir1"
- expired upload sessions
- abandoned raw uploads
- temp normalized audio
- old provider raw response
- transient partial transcript events
- OCR intermediate page images
- redaction intermediate frames
- liveness session media
- biometric artifacts
- old webhook attempts
```

Retention state:

```text id="nqxetb"
ACTIVE
RETENTION_PENDING
LEGAL_HOLD
DELETION_PENDING
DELETED
DELETION_FAILED
```

---

## 35. Security Hardening

Checklist:

```text id="3ypmqu"
[ ] object storage private by default
[ ] signed URLs short-lived
[ ] no public bucket/container
[ ] provider secrets in secret manager/IAM
[ ] no raw text/media in logs
[ ] malware scan if required
[ ] MIME/magic validation
[ ] file size/page/duration limits
[ ] tenant/case authorization
[ ] reviewer access audited
[ ] biometric policy enforced
[ ] raw provider response private
[ ] DB least privilege
[ ] network egress controlled
```

---

## 36. Secrets and IAM

Do not store secrets in DB.

Use:

```text id="mcg661"
- cloud IAM role / workload identity
- Kubernetes Secret only when needed
- external secret manager
- short-lived credentials where possible
- least privilege per worker
```

Example privilege separation:

| Worker | Permission |
|---|---|
| upload API | create upload session, write raw prefix |
| OCR worker | read raw/normalized, write OCR result |
| FFmpeg worker | read source, write derived media |
| TTS worker | write generated audio |
| retention worker | delete allowed prefixes |
| review service | read preview/crop via controlled signed URL |

---

## 38. Alerting Rules

Alert on symptoms.

Examples:

```text id="yeb8c6"
- API submit error rate high
- job queue lag above SLO
- oldest job age too high
- provider error rate high
- provider rate limit high
- worker crashloop
- poison jobs spike
- object storage write failure
- DB connection pool saturated
- retention deletion failed
- review backlog SLA breach
```

Prometheus-style alert example:

```yaml id="wp4hkj"
groups:
  - name: media-ai-alerts
    rules:
      - alert: OcrQueueLagHigh
        expr: media_job_oldest_age_seconds{job_type="OCR"} > 900
        for: 10m
        labels:
          severity: warning
        annotations:
          summary: "OCR queue lag is high"
          description: "Oldest OCR job has been waiting more than 15 minutes."
```

---

## 39. Runbook Example - OCR Queue Lag High

```text id="2gxsml"
Alert:
OCR queue lag > 15 minutes for 10 minutes

Check:
1. Is API still creating jobs?
2. Is ocr-worker running?
3. Worker CPU/memory saturated?
4. Pod crashloop?
5. DB lock/connection issue?
6. Object storage download failing?
7. Provider timeout/rate limit?
8. Poison jobs blocking queue?
9. Recent deployment?

Immediate actions:
1. Scale ocr-worker replicas if CPU-bound and DB/provider OK
2. Pause large low-priority jobs if backlog severe
3. Open provider circuit if provider outage
4. Revert recent release if regression
5. Move poison jobs out of active queue

Follow-up:
- update capacity model
- tune autoscaling
- improve retry classification
- add missing alert if detection was late
```

---

## 40. Incident Playbook - Provider Outage

```text id="jgt0na"
Symptoms:
- provider error rate high
- provider latency p95 high
- many RETRY_PENDING jobs
- queue lag growing

Steps:
1. Confirm provider-specific errors
2. Check provider status page / internal network
3. Open circuit breaker for provider
4. Route to fallback if policy allows
5. For biometric/high-risk jobs, do not provider-shop; queue/review
6. Notify stakeholders
7. Increase polling interval if provider async API degraded
8. Watch cost/retry storm
9. After recovery, half-open provider
10. Reprocess retry queue gradually
```

---

## 41. Incident Playbook - Cost Spike

```text id="kztb7i"
Symptoms:
- provider cost estimate spikes
- audio minutes/pages/chars spike
- duplicate jobs
- TTS cache hit drops
- unusually large files accepted

Steps:
1. Identify tenant/use case/capability
2. Check idempotency hit/miss
3. Check file/page/duration limits
4. Check provider fallback loop
5. Check retry storm
6. Apply quota/backpressure if needed
7. Disable expensive optional features
8. Notify business owner
9. Patch policy or bug
10. Backfill cost attribution
```

---

## 42. Incident Playbook - Biometric Access Anomaly

```text id="wu2hs3"
Symptoms:
- raw biometric access spike
- unusual reviewer access
- access outside case/tenant
- signed URL generation spike
- admin role browsing sensitive assets

Steps:
1. Disable suspicious access path if needed
2. Preserve audit logs
3. Identify actors/assets/subjects
4. Revoke active signed URLs where possible
5. Notify security/privacy lead
6. Check whether data was downloaded/exported
7. Apply legal/compliance process
8. Rotate secrets/keys if needed
9. Review role permissions
10. Add detection rule
```

---

## 43. Capacity Planning

Capacity model per capability:

### OCR

```text id="19405x"
capacity = pages per minute per worker
required_workers = expected_pages_per_minute / worker_pages_per_minute
```

### STT

```text id="xl8sg8"
capacity depends on:
- audio minutes submitted
- provider quota
- polling workers
- result fetch/parse throughput
```

### FFmpeg

```text id="tu8nxp"
capacity = video minutes processed per CPU-hour
depends on profile, resolution, codec, filter, hardware
```

### Face

```text id="susof1"
capacity = images per second per provider/GPU
plus liveness session concurrency
```

### TTS

```text id="x0nk3d"
capacity = characters per second/provider quota
cache hit rate strongly affects cost/capacity
```

---

## 44. Load Test Plan

Test scenarios:

```text id="qrp14x"
1. Upload burst
2. OCR PDF batch
3. Long audio STT
4. Concurrent TTS preview
5. FFmpeg transcode spike
6. Face verification burst
7. Provider outage
8. Object storage latency
9. DB failover
10. Worker pod kill during processing
11. Retry storm
12. Human review backlog
```

Success criteria:

```text id="hn0s4a"
- no lost jobs
- no duplicate provider cost beyond expected
- queue lag recovers
- worker reclaim works
- API remains responsive
- alerts fire
- dashboards explain root cause
```

## 45. Chaos / Resilience Tests

Inject:

```text id="qpnwkg"
- kill worker pod mid-job
- provider timeout
- provider 429
- corrupt media
- object storage 5xx
- DB deadlock
- full temp disk
- slow FFmpeg
- expired signed URL
- Camunda incident
```

Expected behavior:

```text id="ivg9g5"
- retryable failures retry
- permanent failures fail clearly
- poison jobs isolated
- audit event created
- no artifact orphaned without pointer
- no DB state contradiction
```

## 46. Release Safety

For changes to OCR/STT/face/provider:

```text id="a1aawl"
- feature flag
- canary tenant
- shadow mode
- golden fixture tests
- rollback plan
- adapter/normalizer version
- migration script tested
- dashboard watched during rollout
```

Provider/model changes require extra care:

```text id="ya2bkn"
Provider score scales may differ.
Normalizer output may differ.
Threshold may need recalibration.
Human review rate may change.
Cost may change.
```

## 47. DB Maintenance

Expected growth tables:

```text id="3oitue"
media_job
media_job_event
media_asset
ocr_word_result
transcript_word
streaming_transcript_event
audit_event
review_correction
provider_raw_result pointers
```

DB hardening:

```text id="iy3wav"
- partition large event tables by month
- index hot query paths
- archive old event detail
- avoid huge JSONB scans in hot path
- use GIN only where needed
- vacuum/autovacuum tuning
- cap partial streaming event retention
```

Hot indexes:

```sql id="efk3pk"
CREATE INDEX idx_media_job_work_queue
ON media_job (job_type, status, priority DESC, created_at)
WHERE status IN ('SUBMITTED', 'RETRY_PENDING');

CREATE INDEX idx_media_job_tenant_case
ON media_job (tenant_id, case_id, created_at DESC);

CREATE INDEX idx_media_asset_retention
ON media_asset (retention_until)
WHERE lifecycle_status = 'ACTIVE';
```

---

## 48. Object Storage Hygiene

Rules:

```text id="16e6x6"
- raw and derived prefixes separated
- tenant/case/job prefixes
- no original filename as object key
- checksum stored
- object version/etag stored
- lifecycle tags
- legal hold tag if needed
- retention worker reconciles DB and bucket
```

Reconciliation job:

```text id="137yns"
DB says asset ACTIVE but object missing → incident
Object exists but DB missing → quarantine/orphan cleanup
Asset expired but object exists → deletion retry
Provider raw result retained beyond TTL → delete
```

---

## 49. Admin Operations

Admin endpoints should exist but be controlled.

```text id="ee29wd"
POST /admin/media-jobs/{jobId}/retry
POST /admin/media-jobs/{jobId}/cancel
POST /admin/media-jobs/{jobId}/mark-poison
POST /admin/provider-circuits/{provider}/open
POST /admin/provider-circuits/{provider}/half-open
POST /admin/retention-runs
GET  /admin/media-jobs/{jobId}/debug
```

Controls:

```text id="u90mvd"
- admin authz
- step-up auth
- reason required
- audit event
- dry-run where possible
- no raw sensitive data by default
```

```text id="7u2x74"
Architecture
[ ] API and workers separated
[ ] state machine defined

[ ] provider abstraction implemented
[ ] result persistence idempotent
[ ] object storage lifecycle defined

Kubernetes
[ ] resource requests/limits

[ ] probes configured
[ ] graceful shutdown
[ ] worker deployments separated
[ ] GPU node pool if needed
[ ] autoscaling by queue lag/depth

Reliability
[ ] retry classification
[ ] poison job handling
[ ] provider circuit breaker
[ ] fallback matrix
[ ] webhook retry
[ ] worker reclaim after crash
[ ] DB transaction boundaries

Observability
[ ] metrics by job/provider/capability
[ ] structured logs
[ ] distributed tracing
[ ] correlation IDs
[ ] audit events
[ ] dashboards
[ ] actionable alerts

Security
[ ] private storage
[ ] signed URLs short-lived
[ ] secrets not in DB
[ ] least privilege IAM
[ ] no sensitive logs
[ ] malware/MIME validation
[ ] biometric guardrails

Cost
[ ] per-tenant quotas
[ ] cost estimates
[ ] provider cost dashboard
[ ] TTS cache
[ ] duplicate job prevention
[ ] file/page/duration limits

Governance
[ ] retention worker

Ops

[ ] load tests

```

## 51. Minimal Production Baseline

Minimal baseline yang wajib ada sebelum dianggap production-ready:

```text id="s98q0s"
1. Async job model
2. Worker separation
3. Idempotency
4. Retry + poison job
5. Object storage artifact lifecycle
6. Provider abstraction
7. Metrics/logs/traces
8. Queue lag dashboard
9. Cost guardrails
10. Kubernetes requests/limits/probes
11. Retention/deletion worker
12. Admin retry/cancel with audit
13. Incident runbooks
14. Security/privacy guardrails
```

Tanpa ini, sistem masih “prototype yang bisa demo”, belum “platform yang bisa dioperasikan”.

---

## 52. Final Reference Architecture

```text id="snpkx3"
media-api-service
  ├── upload session
  ├── job submission
  ├── job status/result
  ├── signed URL
  └── admin/review API

media-orchestration
  ├── Camunda workflow
  ├── job state transition
  ├── retry/cancel/escalation
  └── human review

media-worker-pool
  ├── metadata-worker
  ├── ocr-worker
  ├── document-ai-worker
  ├── stt-worker
  ├── tts-worker
  ├── face-worker
  ├── ffmpeg-worker
  └── retention-worker

provider-adapter-layer
  ├── AWS
  ├── Azure
  ├── Google
  ├── Tesseract
  ├── FFmpeg
  ├── ONNX/local model
  └── MinIO/S3/Azure Blob

data-layer
  ├── PostgreSQL/MyBatis
  ├── object storage
  ├── audit tables
  ├── result tables
  └── search/vector indexes where needed

ops-layer
  ├── OpenTelemetry
  ├── Prometheus/Grafana
  ├── alerting
  ├── cost dashboard
  ├── runbooks
  └── incident management
```

# Status

**Seri 20/20 selesai. Semua seri untuk topik OCR, Face Recognition, Face ID, Text-to-Speech, Speech-to-Text, Transcribe, dan Media Processing sudah selesai.**

Ringkasan total seri yang sudah selesai:

```text id="ad2wrp"
1. Mental Model & Architecture Overview
2. Media Upload API di Jersey
3. Job State Machine
4. Object Storage & File Lifecycle
5. Media Metadata Extraction
6. Image Preprocessing untuk OCR
7. OCR Basic dengan Tesseract/Tess4J
8. Layout-Aware OCR
9. Document AI Pipeline
10. Human Review Workflow dengan Camunda
11. Speech-to-Text Batch Transcription
12. Streaming Transcription
13. Speaker Diarization, Timestamp Alignment, Subtitle SRT/VTT
14. Text-to-Speech
15. FFmpeg Media Processing
16. Face Detection vs Face Verification vs Face Recognition
17. Face ID, Passkey, WebAuthn, dan Biometric Boundary
18. Biometric Privacy, Consent, Audit, Retention, and Compliance Guardrails
19. Provider Abstraction
20. Production Hardening
```

Rekomendasi lanjutan setelah seri ini: buat **implementation blueprint end-to-end** untuk satu use case nyata, misalnya **“Invoice Document AI Pipeline”** atau **“Video Transcription + Subtitle + Search Pipeline”**, lengkap dengan API, DB schema final, worker flow, Camunda BPMN, dan deployment Kubernetes.
